-eigenbundles
L
or
\overline{L}
is involutive with respect to the Dorfman bracket
\llbracket\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rrbracket.

Remark 3.64
.

This definition is tantamount to a reduction of the structure group
{\mathsf{O}}(2n,2n)
of
E
to
{\sf U}(n,n).
The integrability condition for one of the eigenbundles implies the integrability of the other one, since they are complex conjugate to each other. Then both eigenbundles
L
and
\overline{L}
are Dirac structures, since they are Lagrangian by construction. As discussed in
[
29
]
, generalised complex structures are in one-to-one correspondence with complex Dirac structures satisfying the condition

L\cap\overline{L}=\set{0}\ .

Example 3.65

(
Complex Manifolds
)
.

Let
M
be endowed with an almost complex structure
J.
Then the standard Courant algebroid
\mathbb{T}M=TM\oplus T^{*}M
inherits a generalised almost complex structure

(3.66)

{\mathcal{J}}=\begin{pmatrix}-J&0\\
0&J^{\tt t}\end{pmatrix}

where
J^{\tt t}\colon T^{*}M\to T^{*}M
is the transpose of
J.
The orthogonality of
{\mathcal{J}}
is equivalent to the condition

{\mathcal{J}}^{\tt t}=-{\mathcal{J}}.

Notice that
{\mathcal{J}}
is a generalised complex structure if and only if
J
is a complex structure on
M.
This is shown as follows. The
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
L
of
{\mathcal{J}}
defined in Equation (
3.66
) is given by

(3.67)

\displaystyle L=T_{0,1}\oplus\mathrm{Ann}(T_{0,1})=T_{0,1}\oplus T^{*}_{1,0}\ \subset\ \mathbb{T}M^{\mathbbm{C}}\ ,

which is Lagrangian, where
T_{0,1}
is the
-\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle of
J
given by

(3.68)

\displaystyle T_{0,1}=\set{X+\mathrm{i}\,J(X)\ |\ X\in TM}\ \subset\ TM^{\mathbbm{C}}\ ,

and
T_{1,0}=\overline{T_{0,1}}
is the
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle.
If
L
is involutive, then

(3.69)

\displaystyle\llbracket X+\xi,Y+\eta\rrbracket=[X,Y]+\pounds_{X}\eta-\iota_{Y}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\ \in\ \mathsf{\Gamma}(T_{0,1}\oplus T^{*}_{1,0})

for any
X+\xi,\,Y+\eta\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L),
which implies that
[X,Y]\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(T_{0,1})
for any
X,\,Y\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(T_{0,1}).
Conversely, if
J
is integrable then the Dorfman bracket (
3.69
) can be written as

(3.70)

\displaystyle\llbracket X+\xi,Y+\eta\rrbracket=[X,Y]+\iota_{X}\,\overline{\partial}\eta-\iota_{Y}\,\overline{\partial}\xi

where
\mathrm{d}=\partial+\overline{\partial}
and
\partial
is the Dolbeault differential induced by the integrability of
J.
It follows that
\iota_{X}\,\overline{\partial}\eta-\iota_{Y}\,\overline{\partial}\xi\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(T^{*}_{1,0})
by definition of
\partial,

and hence
L
is involutive with respect to the Dorfman bracket.

Example 3.71

(
Symplectic Manifolds
)
.

Let
(M,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold. Then
\omega
determines a generalised complex structure on
\mathbb{T}M
given by

(3.72)

{\mathcal{J}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\omega^{-1}\\
\omega&0\end{pmatrix}

whose
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle is

(3.73)

\displaystyle L=\set{X-\mathrm{i}\,\iota_{X}\omega\ |\ X\in TM}\ \subset\ \mathbb{T}M^{\mathbbm{C}}\ .

One shows that
L
is involutive by an identical calculation to that of Example
3.45
.

Let
L\subset E^{\mathbbm{C}}
be a Lagrangian subbundle. Recall from Remark
2.11
that, at each point
m\in M
, there is a pure spinor line
\Omega_{m}
associated to the fibre
L_{m}
given by

(3.74)

\displaystyle\Omega_{m}=\set{\psi\in{\mathsf{S}}_{m}^{\mathbbm{C}}\ |\ \Psi(e)\psi=0\ ,\ \forall\,e\in L_{m}}\ .

This defines a complex line bundle
\Omega
over
M
, which equivalently defines a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
when
L\cap\overline{L}=\set{0}
. We may thus interchangably use
{\mathcal{J}}
,
L
or
\Omega
when discussing generalised complex structures. The pure spinor line bundle
\Omega
defines a filtration of
\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M^{\mathbbm{C}}
, where
\Omega^{n}=\Omega
and

\Omega^{n-k}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k}\overline{L}\cdot\Omega^{n}

for
k=1,\dots,n
; we set
\Omega^{k}=\set{0}
for
k<0
and
k>n
. If
L
defines a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
, this becomes a grading

(3.75)

\displaystyle\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M^{\mathbbm{C}}=\Omega^{n}\oplus\Omega^{n-1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\Omega^{0}

since then
L\cap\overline{L}=\set{0}
.

Let
H\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{\tt cl}^{3}(M)
be a closed three-form. The
H
-twisted integrability of a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
on
\mathbb{T}M
(or a Lagrangian subbbundle in general) can be formulated in terms of the Dirac generating operator
\mathrm{d}_{H}
: by
[
29
, Theorem 3.38]
,
{\mathcal{J}}
is integrable if and only if

\mathrm{d}_{H}\big({\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\Omega^{n})\big)\ \subset\ \mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{n-1})\ .

If
{\mathcal{J}}
is integrable, then
\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k}))\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k-1})\oplus\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k+1})
for each
k\in\set{0,\dots,n}
, and there is a decomposition
\mathrm{d}_{H}=\partial_{H}+\overline{\partial}_{H}
where
[
29
, Theorem 4.23]

(3.76)

\displaystyle\partial_{H}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k-1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{\partial}_{H}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega^{k+1})\ .

There is also the notion of the type of a generalised complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
on a real
2n
-dimensional manifold
M
, which returns an integer
k\in\set{0,\dots,n}
at each point, and defines a lower semicontinuous function on
M
.

Definition 3.77
.

The
type
of
{\mathcal{J}}
, denoted
{\rm type}({\mathcal{J}})
, is the complex codimension of
\rho(L)
in
TM^{\mathbbm{C}}
:

{\rm type}({\mathcal{J}})=n-{\rm rk}_{\mathbbm{C}}\big(\rho(L)\big)\ .

In the case of the standard Courant algebroid, for any
m\in M
an element
\psi
of
\Omega_{m}
can be written as
[
29
, Theorem 4.8]

(3.78)

\displaystyle\psi=\exp(B+\mathrm{i}\,\omega)\wedge\Theta

for real two-forms
B
and
\omega
, and
\Theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{k}
for some linearly independent complex one-forms
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{k}
. The condition that
L\cap\overline{L}=\set{0}
can be formulated as the requirement that

\omega^{n-k}\wedge\Theta\wedge\overline{\Theta}\neq 0\ .

The integer
k={\rm type}({\mathcal{J}})
is the type of the generalised complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
.

3.3.1.
Reduction of Generalised Complex Structures

In this subsection we assume that the exact Courant algebroid
E\to M
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
3.31
.

Proposition 3.79

(
Generalised Complex Reduction
)
.

Let
E
be a reducible exact Courant algebroid over
M
endowed with a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}},
with
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
L\subset E^{\mathbbm{C}},
such that
{\mathcal{J}}(K)\cap K^{\perp}\subset K
has constant rank. Suppose that

(3.80)

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}(K^{\mathbbm{C}}),\mathsf{\Gamma}(L\cap K^{\perp}{}^{\mathbbm{C}})\rrbracket\ \subset\ \mathsf{\Gamma}(L+K^{\mathbbm{C}})\ .

Then the reduced Courant algebroid
\underline{E}
over
{\mathcal{Q}}
inherits a generalised almost complex structure
\underline{{\mathcal{J}}}
with
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle

\underline{L}=\natural(L\cap K^{\perp}{}^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}})\ .

Proof.

By Proposition
3.49
, the condition (
3.80
) guarantees the existence of the subbundle
\underline{L}\subset\underline{E}^{\mathbbm{C}}.
In order to show that
\underline{L}
is the
+\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle of a generalised almost complex structure, we need
\underline{L}\cap\overline{\underline{L}}=\set{0}.
This follows from the assumptions that
{\mathcal{J}}(K)\cap K^{\perp}\subset K

has constant rank by using
[
10
, Lemma 5.1]
.
∎

3.4.
Generalised Kähler Structures

We now introduce the notion of generalised Kähler structures on Courant algebroids. First, we define generalised metrics.

Definition 3.81

(
Generalised Metrics
)
.

A
generalised metric
on a Courant algebroid
E
is an automorphism
\tau\in\mathsf{Aut}(E)
covering the identity with
\tau^{2}=\mathds{1}
which, together with the pairing
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle
, defines a positive-definite fibrewise metric on
E
given by

(3.82)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}(e,e^{\prime})=\langle e,\tau(e^{\prime})\rangle\ ,

for
e,e^{\prime}\in E
. Equivalently, a generalised metric on
E
is a maximally positive-definite subbundle
V^{+}\subset E
(with respect to
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle
); the subbundles
V^{+}
and
V^{-}\coloneqq(V^{+})^{\perp}
give an orthogonal decomposition

E=V^{+}\oplus V^{-}

into the
\pm 1
-eigenbundles

(3.83)

\displaystyle V^{\pm}=\ker(\tau\mp\mathds{1})\ .

Remark 3.84
.

If
E
is an exact Courant algebroid, then the restriction of the anchor to
V^{+}
is an isomorphism
\rho|_{V^{+}}:V^{+}\to TM
. In a splitting
E\simeq\mathbb{T}M
, any generalised metric
V^{+}
can be written as

(3.85)

\displaystyle V^{+}=\text{gr}(g+b)=\{X+\iota_{X}g+\iota_{X}b\ |\ X\in TM\}\ ,

for a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
and a Kalb-Ramond field
b\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{2}(M)
. The complement
V^{-}
is then given by
V^{-}=\text{gr}(-g+b).

Definition 3.86

(
Generalised Kähler Structures
)
.

Let
E\to M
be an exact Courant algebroid with
\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(E)=4n
, where
\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}(M)=2n
. A
generalised almost Kähler structure
on
E
is a pair of generalised almost complex structures
({\mathcal{J}}^{+},{\mathcal{J}}^{-})
such that

(1)

{\mathcal{J}}^{+}\,{\mathcal{J}}^{-}={\mathcal{J}}^{-}\,{\mathcal{J}}^{+}\ ,
and

(2)

\tau=-{\mathcal{J}}^{+}\,{\mathcal{J}}^{-}
defines a generalised metric.

We sometimes denote the pair
({\mathcal{J}}^{+},{\mathcal{J}}^{-})
by
{\mathcal{K}}
. A generalised almost Kähler structure
{\mathcal{K}}=({\mathcal{J}}^{+},{\mathcal{J}}^{-})
is
integrable
or a
generalised Kähler structure
when both
{\mathcal{J}}^{\pm}
are generalised complex structures.

A
generalised Calabi-Yau structure
on
E
is a generalised Kähler structure whose associated pure spinor line bundles are holomorphically trivial, i.e. there are nowhere vanishing sections
\psi^{\pm}
of the line bundles
\Omega^{\pm}
associated to
{\mathcal{J}}^{\pm}
such that

\mathrm{d}_{H}\psi^{\pm}=0\ .

The Mukai pairings on these sections are required to be related as

(3.87)

\displaystyle(\!(\psi^{+},\overline{\psi^{+}}\,)\!)=\pm\,(\!(\psi^{-},\overline{\psi^{-}}\,)\!)\ .

Example 3.88

(
Kähler Manifolds
)
.

A classical Kähler structure on a manifold
M
is a triple
(g,J,\omega)
of a Riemannian metric
g
, a complex structure
J
and a symplectic form
\omega
such that
\omega=g\,J
. Taking
{\mathcal{J}}^{+}={\mathcal{J}}_{J}
(Example
3.65
) and
{\mathcal{J}}^{-}={\mathcal{J}}_{\omega}
(Example
3.71
), it follows that
({\mathcal{J}}^{+},{\mathcal{J}}^{-})
is a generalised Kähler structure on
(\mathbb{T}M,0)
.

If the classical Kähler structure is a Calabi-Yau structure, then the associated holomorphic volume form
\Theta
and Kähler form
\omega
satisfy

\omega^{n}=\frac{\mathrm{i}^{n}\,n!}{2^{n}}\ \Theta\wedge\overline{\Theta}\ ,

and hence define a generalised Calabi-Yau structure with

(3.89)

\displaystyle\psi^{+}=\Theta\qquad\text{and}\qquad\psi^{-}=\exp(\,\mathrm{i}\,\omega)\ .

Here
\mathrm{type}({\mathcal{J}}^{+})=n
whereas
\mathrm{type}({\mathcal{J}}^{-})=0
.

We shall now discuss the link, shown in
[
29
]
, between generalised Kähler structures and bi-Hermitian structures.

Definition 3.90

(
Bi-Hermitian Structures
)
.

A
bi-Hermitian structure
on a real
2n
-dimensional manifold
M
is a triple
(g,J^{+},J^{-})
of a Riemannian metric
g
and two complex structures
J^{\pm}
, such that both pairs
(g,J^{+})
and
(g,J^{-})
are Hermitian structures on
M
. A
bi-Hermitian structure with
b
-field
on
M
is a quadruple
(g,b,J^{+},J^{-})
of a bi-Hermitian structure
(g,J^{+},J^{-})
and a Kalb-Ramond field
b\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{2}(M)
, such that the fundamental two-forms
\omega_{\pm}=g\,J^{\pm}
satisfy

(3.91)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{d}^{c}_{+}\omega_{+}=\mathrm{d}^{c}_{-}\omega_{-}=\mathrm{d}b\ ,

where
\mathrm{d}^{c}_{\pm}\coloneqq J^{\pm}{}^{-1}\circ\mathrm{d}\circ J^{\pm}
. A bi-Hermitian structure with
b
-field
(g,b,J^{+},J^{-})
is
H
-twisted
if the fundamental two-forms satisfy

(3.92)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{d}^{c}_{+}\omega_{+}=\mathrm{d}^{c}_{-}\omega_{-}=H+\mathrm{d}b\ ,

for some
H\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{3}_{\tt cl}(M)
.

All of these bi-Hermitian structures are
{\mathsf{U}}(n){\times}{\mathsf{U}}(n)
-structures on
M
. In the following we will refer to all of them simply as bi-Hermitian structures for the sake of brevity.

An equivalent definition of generalised Kähler structures is now provided by

Theorem 3.93

(​​​
[
29
, Theorem 6.37]
)
.

Let
M
be a real
2n
-manifold and take a closed three-form
H\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{\tt cl}^{3}(M)
. The bi-Hermitian structure
(g,b,J^{+},J^{-})
of a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
, a Kalb-Ramond field
b\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{2}(M)
, and two complex structures
J^{\pm}
on
M
preserving
g
is equivalent to a generalised almost Kähler structure by

(3.94)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}\,\begin{pmatrix}\mathds{1}&0\\
b&\mathds{1}\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}J^{+}\pm J^{-}&-(\omega_{+}^{-1}\mp\omega_{-}^{-1})\\
\omega_{+}\mp\omega_{-}&-(J^{+}{}^{\tt t}\pm J^{-}{}^{\tt t})\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}\mathds{1}&0\\
-b&\mathds{1}\end{pmatrix}\ .

Then
{\mathcal{K}}=({\mathcal{J}}^{+},{\mathcal{J}}^{-})
is a generalised Kähler structure on
(\mathbb{T}M,H)
if and only if Equation (
3.92
) is satisfied.

This result shows that there is a one-to-one correspondence between generalised Kähler structures on
M
and bi-Hermitian structures on
M
. The first instance in which generalised Kähler structures appeared, in the form of bi-Hermitian structures, was in
[
28
]
where the action functional of a two-dimensional
{\mathcal{N}}=(2,2)
sigma-model is written using the parametrisation
(g,b)
of the generalised metric
\tau=-{\mathcal{J}}^{+}\,{\mathcal{J}}^{-}
and the closed three-form
H
. The condition that this action functional is invariant under
{\mathcal{N}}=(2,2)
supersymmetry transformations is equivalent to Equation (
3.92
).

4.
Courant Algebroid and Dirac Relations

Building on the notions of linear relations from Section
2.1
, in this section we briefly review Courant algebroid relations and their uses in setting up a notion of T-duality, following
[
55
,
21
]
. We then use this to give a suitable notion of relation between Dirac structures, and establish conditions under which Dirac structures are related by T-duality.

4.1.
Courant Algebroid Relations

We give a brief introduction to Courant algebroid relations.
For two Courant algebroids
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
over smooth manifolds
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, respectively, the product

\big(E_{1}\times E_{2}\,,\,\llbracket\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rrbracket\,,\,\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle\,,\,\rho\big)

is a Courant algebroid over
M_{1}\times M_{2}
, with the Courant algebroid structures defined by

(4.1)

\displaystyle\rho(e_{1},e_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big(\rho_{E_{1}}(e_{1}),\rho_{E_{2}}(e_{2})\big)\ ,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\langle(e_{1},e_{2})\,,\,(e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle\coloneqq\langle e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rangle_{E_{1}}\circ\mathtt{p}_{1}+\langle e_{2},e_{2}^{\prime}\rangle_{E_{2}}\circ\mathtt{p}_{2}\ ,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\llbracket(e_{1},e_{2})\,,\,(e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime})\rrbracket

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big(\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{E_{1}}\,,\,\llbracket e_{2},e_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{E_{2}}\big)\ ,

where
\mathtt{p}_{i}\colon M_{1}\times M_{2}\to M_{i}
are the projection maps for
i=1,2
; for the sake of brevity, we will usually not write the projections explicitly in the following.
Denote by
\overline{E}_{i}
the Courant algebroid
(E_{i},\llbracket\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rrbracket_{E_{i}},-\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{E_{i}},\rho_{E_{i}})
.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
be Courant algebroids over
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
respectively.

A
Courant algebroid relation from
E_{1}
to
E_{2}
is a Dirac structure
R\subseteq E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
supported on a submanifold
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
such that
\rho(R^{\perp})\subset TC,
denoted
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}.

If
C
is the graph of a smooth map
\varphi\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}
, then
R
is a
Courant algebroid morphism from
E_{1}
to
E_{2}
over
\varphi
, denoted
R\colon E_{1}\rightarrowtail E_{2}
.

If
R
is further the graph of some vector bundle map
\Phi\colon E_{1}\to E_{2}
covering
\varphi\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}
, then
\Phi
is a
classical Courant algebroid morphism over
\varphi
.

By viewing a Courant algebroid relation
R
as a subbundle of
E_{2}\times\overline{E}_{1}
, we obtain the
transpose relation

R^{\top}\colon E_{2}\dashrightarrow E_{1}
, whose support is denoted
C^{\top}\subseteq M_{2}\times M_{1}
, and similarly the
transpose morphism

R^{\top}\colon E_{2}\rightarrowtail E_{1}
when
\varphi
is a diffeomorphism.

We will also need a notion of sections related by a Courant algebroid relation. If
E
is a Courant algebroid over
M
and
L\subset E
is a subbundle supported on a submanifold
C\subset M
, denote by
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(E;L)
the
C^{\infty}(M)
-submodule of sections of
E
which take values in
L
when restricted to
C
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation supported on
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
. Two sections
e_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(E_{1})
and
e_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(E_{2})
are
R
-
related
, denoted
e_{1}\sim_{R}e_{2},
if
(e_{1},e_{2})\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2};R).

Following Definition
2.7
we introduce

Definition 4.6
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation supported on
C
. The
kernel
and
cokernel
of
R
are given by

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\rm K}_{R}\coloneqq(E_{1}\times 0_{E_{2}})\big\rvert_{C}\cap R\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\rm CK}_{R}\coloneqq(0_{E_{1}}\times E_{2})\big\rvert_{C}\cap R\ ,

respectively, where
0_{E_{i}}
is the zero section of
E_{i}
, for
i=1,2
. Note that both
{\rm K}_{R}
and
{\rm CK}_{R}
are singular subbundles of
R
. We also denote
\ker(R)=\mathtt{p}_{1}({\rm K}_{R})
and
{\rm coker}(R)=\mathtt{p}_{2}({\rm CK}_{R})
.

Definition
4.6
may be formulated more generally for any isotropic subbundle
R
of
E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
, where
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are pseudo-Euclidean vector bundles.

Example 4.8

(
Reduction of Courant Algebroids
)
.

By
[
21
, Proposition 3.7]
, the reduction of a Courant algebroid in Theorem
3.31

gives the Courant algebroid morphism

(4.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(K):E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{E}}

supported on
\text{gr}(\varpi)\subset M\times{\mathcal{Q}},

where
Q(K)
is defined pointwise by

(4.10)

\displaystyle Q(K)_{(m,\varpi(m))}=\set{(e,\natural(e))\,}{\,e\in K^{\perp}_{m}}\ \subset\ E_{m}\times\underline{\overline{E}}{}_{\varpi(m)}\ ,

for any
m\in M.
Its kernel and cokernel are

{\rm K}_{Q(K)}=(K\times 0_{\underline{E}})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\rm CK}_{Q(K)}=(0_{E_{1}}\times 0_{E_{2}})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}=0_{Q(K)}\ .

A particularly relevant class of Courant algebroid relations is given by

Definition 4.11

(
Generalised Isometries
)
.

Suppose
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
is a Courant algebroid relation supported on a submanifold
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
. Let
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
be generalised metrics for
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
respectively. Then
R
is a
generalised isometry between
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
if

(\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2})(R)=R\ .

Equivalently, if
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
are the maximally positive-definite subbundles associated to
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
respectively, then
R
is a generalised isometry if

(4.12)

\displaystyle R_{c}=R_{c}^{+}\oplus R^{-}_{c}

for every
c\in C
, where

R_{c}^{\pm}=R_{c}\cap\big(V_{1}^{\pm}\times V_{2}^{\pm}\big)_{c}\ .

As proved in
[
21
, Proposition 4.17]
, the kernel and cokernel of a generalised isometry
R
are trivial, i.e.
{\rm K}_{R}={\rm CK}_{R}={0_{R}}
.

Let us discuss how Courant algebroid relations compose.

Definition 4.13
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
and
R^{\prime}\colon E_{2}\dashrightarrow E_{3}
be Courant algebroid relations supported on
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
and
C^{\prime}\subseteq M_{2}\times M_{3}
respectively. The
composition

R^{\prime}\circ R
is the subset of
E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{3}
given by

(4.14)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptR^{\prime}\circ R=\set{(e_{1},e_{3})\in E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{3}\,}{\,(e_{1},e_{2})\in R\ ,\ (e_{2},e_{3})\in R^{\prime}\ \text{ for some }e_{2}\in E_{2}}\ .

The composition is a Courant algebroid relation
R^{\prime}\circ R:E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{3}
supported on
C^{\prime}\circ C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{3}
if
R
and
R^{\prime}
compose cleanly, see
[
55
,
21
]
for more details.

4.2.
Relating Dirac Structures

We introduce the notion of relation between Dirac structures descending from Courant algebroid relations.

Definition 4.15

(
Dirac Relations
)
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation supported on a submanifold
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
. Let
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
be (almost) Dirac structures for
E_{1}
and
E_{2},
respectively. The restriction of
R
to
L_{i}
for
i=1,2
is given by

(4.16)

\displaystyle R\big\rvert_{L_{i}}\coloneqq\set{(e_{1},e_{2})\in R\ |\ e_{i}\in L_{i}}\ .

Then
R
is a
relation between the
(
almost
)
Dirac structures
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, or a (
almost
)
Dirac relation
, if

(4.17)

\displaystyle R\big\rvert_{L_{1}}=R\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})\big\rvert_{C}+{\rm CK}_{R}\qquad\text{and}\qquad R\big\rvert_{L_{2}}=R\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})\big\rvert_{C}+{\rm K}_{R}

are regular vector subbundles supported on
C,
and we write
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
. The inclusion
\supseteq
always holds.

Remark 4.18

(
Dirac vs. Lie Algebroid Relations
)
.

Let
(A_{1},[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]_{1},\rho_{1})
and
(A_{2},[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]_{2},\rho_{2})
be Lie algebroids over
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, respectively. Then the product vector bundle
A_{1}\times A_{2}
over
M_{1}\times M_{2}
is endowed with a Lie algebroid structure given by the Lie bracket
([\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]_{1},[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]_{2})
and anchor map
(\rho_{1},\rho_{2}).

A
Lie algebroid relation

R
between the Lie algebroids
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
is a Lie subalgebroid
R\subseteq A_{1}\times A_{2}
supported on a submanifold
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
. This generalises the notion of Lie algebroid morphism over a smooth map
\varphi:M_{1}\to M_{2}
where
C=\text{gr}(\varphi)
, see e.g.
[
43
, Section 2.3]
.

Suppose
{\rm CK_{R}}={\rm K}_{R}={0_{R}}
in Definition
4.15
, so that
R\rvert_{L_{1}}=R\rvert_{L_{2}}
. If
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are Dirac structures, then the Dirac relation
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
is a Lie algebroid relation between
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
. Recall that the Lie algebroid structures for
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are induced by the Courant algebroid structures on
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
respectively. We may motivate our definition of Dirac relation as a way to generalise morphisms of Dirac structures as descending from the “arrows” between the Courant algebroids they live in, rather than just considering their Lie algebroid structure.

Example 4.19

(
Foliated Manifolds
)
.

Let
(M_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{1})
and
(M_{2},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
be foliated manifolds and consider a foliation-preserving diffeomorphism
\varphi\in\mathsf{Diff}(M_{1},M_{2}),
i.e.
\varphi_{*}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{1})=T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
. Then
\varphi
induces the Courant algebroid isomorphism

(4.20)

\displaystyle\bm{\varphi}\coloneqq\varphi_{*}\oplus(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\colon(\mathbb{T}M_{1},0)\longrightarrow(\mathbb{T}M_{2},0)

covering
\varphi.
We show that the induced Courant algebroid relation
\text{gr}(\bm{\varphi})
supported on
\text{gr}(\varphi)
is a Dirac relation between the Dirac structures
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
induced by the foliations, where

(4.21)

\displaystyle L_{i}\coloneqq T{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\oplus\mathrm{Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{i})\ ,

for
i=1,2.

In this case

(4.22)

\displaystyle\text{gr}(\bm{\varphi})\big\rvert_{L_{1}}=\set{(X+\alpha,\varphi_{*}(X)+(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(\alpha))\ |\ X+\alpha\in T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\oplus\mathrm{Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{1})}\ .

Since
\varphi_{*}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{1})=T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
it follows that

\text{gr}(\bm{\varphi})\rvert_{L_{1}}\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}
is a subbundle of
L_{1}\times L_{2}
supported on
\text{gr}(\varphi),
i.e.
\text{gr}(\bm{\varphi})
is a Dirac relation. Furthermore,
\text{gr}(\bm{\varphi})\rvert_{L_{1}}
is a Lie subalgebroid of
L_{1}\times L_{2}
supported on
\text{gr}(\varphi)
since

(4.23)

\displaystyle\bm{\varphi}(\llbracket X+\alpha,Y+\beta\rrbracket_{L_{1}})=\llbracket\bm{\varphi}(X+\alpha),\bm{\varphi}(Y+\beta)\rrbracket_{L_{2}}

for all
X+\alpha,Y+\beta\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{1}),
as is easily verified by applying
\bm{\varphi}
to the bracket (
3.44
).

Example 4.24

(
Dirac Reduction
)
.

Consider the setting of Example
4.8
, i.e. let
Q(K)
be the reduction relation of a Courant algebroid
E
by an isotropic subbundle
K
with reduced Courant algebroid
\underline{E}
. We also assume to be in the setting of Subsection
3.2.1
, i.e.
E
is endowed with a Dirac structure
L
that reduces to the Dirac structure

\underline{L}=\natural(L\cap K^{\perp}+K)\ .

We shall show first that
Q(K)
is a Dirac relation between
L
and
\underline{L}
in the sense of Definition
4.15
. For each
m\in M
consider

(4.25)

\displaystyle{Q(K)\big\rvert_{L}}_{(m,\varpi(m))}=\set{(e,\natural(e))\ |\ e\in K^{\perp}_{m}\cap L_{m}}\ .

This is a vector subspace of
L_{m}\times{\underline{L}}_{\varpi(m)}
because the image of
\natural
restricted to
K^{\perp}\cap L+K
is
\underline{L},
as shown in Subsection
3.2.1
. For
L
to be reducible,
K^{\perp}\cap L
has to have constant rank, which implies that

Q(K)\big\rvert_{L}\ \subset\ (L\times{\underline{L}})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}

is a regular vector subbundle supported on
\text{gr}(\varpi).
Note that
{\rm CK}_{Q(K)}=0_{Q(K)}
by Example
4.8
.

Similarly

(4.26)

\displaystyle{Q(K)\big\rvert_{\underline{L}}}_{(m,\varpi(m))}=\set{(e,\natural(e))\in Q(K)_{(m,\varpi(m))}\ |\ \natural(e)\in{\underline{L}}_{\varpi(m)}}\ ,

where
e\in K^{\perp}_{m}\cap L_{m}+K_{m}
by definition of
\natural
and
\underline{L}
. Again by the regularity of
K^{\perp}\cap L
it follows that

Q(K)\big|_{\underline{L}}\ \subset\ (L\times\underline{L})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}+{\rm K}_{Q(K)}

is a regular vector subbundle, thus showing that
L\sim_{Q(K)}\underline{L}
.

Lastly,
Q(K)\rvert_{L}
is a “bracket morphism” between
L
and
\underline{L},
as shown in Equation (
3.54
). The anchors are related because
Q(K)
is a Courant algebroid relation. Thus it represents a Lie algebroid relation between
L
and
\underline{L}.
Hence
Q(K)
is a Dirac relation between
L
and
\underline{L}.

4.2.1.
Morphisms of Manin Pairs

Let us make contact with the existing literature concerning Courant algebroid relations between Dirac structures. We focus primarily on morphisms of Manin pairs for Courant algebroids, formulated in
[
12
]
.
A
Manin pair

(E,L)
is given by a Courant algebroid
E
over
M
endowed with a Dirac structure
L
supported on
M.

Definition 4.27
.

Let
(E_{1},L_{1})
and
(E_{2},L_{2})
be Manin pairs over
M_{1}
and
M_{2},
respectively. Let
\varphi\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}
be a smooth map. A
morphism of Manin pairs
between
(E_{1},L_{1})
and
(E_{2},L_{2})
is a Dirac structure
D\subset E_{1}\times E_{2}
supported on
\text{gr}(\varphi)\subset M_{1}\times M_{2}
such that
D\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(L_{1})=\set{0}
and
D\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(L_{1}\oplus\varphi^{*}E_{2})
projects onto
\varphi^{*}L_{2}
under the natural projection
E_{1}\oplus\varphi^{*}E_{2}\to\varphi^{*}E_{2}.

The latter projection becomes an isomorphism onto
\varphi^{*}L_{2}
when restricted to
D\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(L_{1}\oplus\varphi^{*}E_{2}).

Proposition 4.28
.

A morphism
D
of Manin pairs
(E_{1},L_{1})
and
(E_{2},L_{2})
is a Dirac relation in the sense of Definition
4.15
.

Proof.

If
D\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(L_{1}\oplus\varphi^{*}E_{2})
projects onto
\varphi^{*}L_{2}
then

(4.29)

\displaystyle D\cap(L_{1}\times E_{2})=D\big|_{L_{1}}=D\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})\ .

It follows that
{\rm CK}_{D}=0_{D}
. Since the projection
D\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(L_{1}\oplus\varphi^{*}E_{2})\to\varphi^{*}L_{2}
is onto, it follows that for every
e_{2}\in L_{2}
there is some
e_{1}\in E_{1}
such that
(e_{1},e_{2})\in D
, and hence

D\big|_{L_{2}}=D\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})+{\rm K}_{D}\ ,

as required.
∎

A particularly relevant example of morphism of Manin pairs arises from

Definition 4.30
.

Let
M
and
N
be smooth manifolds with a smooth map
f\colon M\to N
. For any
m\in M,
elements
X+\alpha\in\mathbb{T}_{m}M
and
Y+\beta\in\mathbb{T}_{f(m)}N
are
f
-
related
if
Y=(f_{*})_{m}(X)
and
\alpha=f^{*}_{m}(\beta).
We write
(X+\alpha)\sim_{f}(Y+\beta).

Lemma 4.31
.

Let
(\mathbb{T}M,f^{*}H)
and
(\mathbb{T}N,H)
be twisted standard Courant algebroids, where
H\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{\tt cl}^{3}(N)
and
f\colon M\to N
is a smooth map. If
X_{i}+\alpha_{i}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\mathbb{T}M)
is
f
-related to
Y_{i}+\beta_{i}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\mathbb{T}N),
for
i=1,2,
then

(4.32)

\displaystyle\langle X_{1}+\alpha_{1},X_{2}+\alpha_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}M}=\langle Y_{1}+\beta_{1},Y_{2}+\beta_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}N}\ ,

and

(4.33)

\displaystyle\llbracket X_{1}+\alpha_{1},X_{2}+\alpha_{2}\rrbracket_{f^{*}H}=\llbracket Y_{1}+\beta_{1},Y_{2}+\beta_{2}\rrbracket_{H}\ .

Proof.

See
[
51
]
.
∎

An immediate consequence of Lemma
4.31
is thus

Proposition 4.34
.

The subbundle

(4.35)

\displaystyle R_{f}\coloneqq\set{\left(X+\alpha,Y+\beta\right)\in\mathbb{T}M\times\mathbb{T}N\ |\ (X+\alpha)\sim_{f}(Y+\beta)}

is a a Courant algebroid relation
R_{f}:(\mathbb{T}M,f^{*}H)\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}N,H)
supported on
\text{gr}(f)
.

As in
[
12
, Example 2.11]
, by taking any Dirac structures
L\subset\mathbb{T}M
and
L^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{T}N
, it follows that that
R_{f}
induces a Dirac relation if and only if

(4.36)

\displaystyle\set{f_{*}(X)+\alpha\ }{\ X\in TM\ ,\ \alpha\in T^{*}N\ ,\ X+f^{*}(\alpha)\in L}

\displaystyle=L^{\prime}\ ,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}\ker(\mathrm{d}f)\cap\mathtt{p}_{1}^{*}(TM)\cap L

\displaystyle=\set{0}\ ,

that is,
f
is a
strong Dirac map
(see
[
12
]
and references therein for details).

4.3.
The T-Duality Relation

Let
E
be an exact Courant algebroid over
M
endowed with isotropic subbundles
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
such that

\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(K_{1})=\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(K_{2})
.
Assume that
K_{1}^{\perp}
and
K_{2}^{\perp}
are pointwise spanned by basic sections, and denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
the foliation of
M
induced by the integrable distribution
\rho(K_{i}).
Suppose that the leaf spaces
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}=M/{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
have a smooth structure, hence there are unique surjective submersions
\varpi_{i}\colon M\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
for
i=1,2,
which we summarise in the bisubmersion diagram

(4.38)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{2}}

We consider the setting in which the diagram

(4.39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{E}{}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{E}{}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(K_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(K_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}

defines a Courant algebroid relation

(4.40)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R=Q(K_{2})\circ Q(K_{1})^{\top}\ \colon\ \underline{E}{}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{E}{}_{2}}

supported on the submanifold

(4.41)

\displaystyle{\underline{C}}=\set{(\varpi_{1}(m),\varpi_{2}(m))\,}{\,m\in M}\ \subset\ {\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\ .

It was shown in
[
21
, Theorem 5.8]
that if
\underline{C}
is a smooth manifold and
T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
has constant rank, then
R
is a Courant algebroid relation if and only if
K_{1}\cap K_{2}
has constant rank. Then
R
is a Dirac structure in
\underline{E}{}_{1}\times\overline{\underline{E}}{}_{2}
supported on
\underline{C}\,
, which is given explicitly by

(4.42)

\displaystyle R=\set{(\natural_{1}(e),\natural_{2}(e))\,}{\,e\in K_{1}^{\perp}\cap K_{2}^{\perp}}\ ,

where
\natural_{i}\colon K_{i}^{\perp}\to\bigr(K_{i}^{\perp}/K_{i}\bigl)/{\mathcal{F}}_{i}=\underline{E}{}_{i}
denotes the quotient map for
i=1,2
.
It was shown in
[
21
, Lemma 5.7]
that
\underline{C}
is a smooth manifold if
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are fibred over the same manifold
{\mathcal{B}},
in which case
\underline{C}
is the fibred product
\underline{C}={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}.

In this instance
R
is said to be a
T-duality relation
between the reduced Courant algebroids
\underline{E}{}_{1}
and
\underline{E}{}_{2}

[
21
]
. It describes a topological T-duality between the manifolds
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
(generically endowed with NS–NS fluxes). Geometric T-duality is defined in
[
21
]
by introducing generalised metrics
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
, or equivalently
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
, into this setting.

Definition 4.43
.

Two Courant algebroids endowed with generalised metrics
(\underline{E}{}_{1},V_{1}^{+})
and
(\underline{E}{}_{2},V_{2}^{+})
fitting into Diagram (
4.39
) are
geometrically T-dual
if
R
is a generalised isometry:
(\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2})(R)=R
.

The problem of transporting a generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}\subset\underline{E}{}_{1}
to
\underline{E}{}_{2}
such that
R
becomes a generalised isometry is addressed in
[
21
]
.
For this, introduce the subbundle

D_{1}\coloneqq T{\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}}{}_{1}=\varpi_{1*}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

of
T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, which is induced by the subbundle
K_{2}\subset E
and called the
distribution of T-duality directions
.
It plays the role of the bundle of isometries over
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
.

Definition 4.44
.

Let
\underline{\sigma}_{1}\colon T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to\underline{E}{}_{1}
be the splitting of the exact Courant algebroid
\underline{E}{}_{1}
over
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
induced by an isotropic splitting
\sigma_{1}\colon TM\to E
of
E
which is adapted to
K_{1}\subset E
.
A generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
is
invariant with respect to
D_{1}
, or simply
D_{1}
-invariant
, if there are generators
\set{\underline{X}{}_{1},\dots,\underline{X}{}_{r_{1}}}\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
of the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
-submodule
\mathsf{\Gamma}(D_{1})
such that

(4.45)

\displaystyle\mathsf{ad}^{\underline{\sigma}_{1}}_{\underline{X}{}_{k}}(\underline{w}{}_{1})\ \in\ \mathsf{\Gamma}(V_{1}^{+})\ ,

for
k=1,\dots,r_{1}
and
\underline{w}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(V_{1}^{+})
. The Lie subalgebra

\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}}\coloneqq\text{Span}_{\mathbbm{R}}\set{\underline{X}{}_{1},\dots,\underline{X}{}_{r_{1}}}

of
\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
is the
isometry algebra of
D_{1}
or the
Lie algebra of Killing vector fields for
V_{1}^{+}
.

Remark 4.46
.

For a twisted standard Courant algebroid
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})
with any
\underline{X}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
,
\underline{e}=\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
\underline{\omega}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
, one computes

(4.47)

\displaystyle\pounds_{\underline{X}}\big((\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta})\cdot\underline{\omega}\big)

\displaystyle=\pounds_{\underline{X}}(\iota_{\underline{Y}}\omega)+\pounds_{\underline{X}}(\underline{\eta}\wedge\underline{\omega}\big)

(4.48)

\displaystyle=\iota_{\underline{Y}}\,\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\omega}+\iota_{[\underline{X},\underline{Y}]}\underline{\omega}+(\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\eta})\wedge\underline{\omega}+\underline{\eta}\wedge\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\omega}

(4.49)

\displaystyle=(\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta})\cdot\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\omega}+([\underline{X},\underline{Y}]+\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\eta})\cdot\underline{\omega}\ ,

which implies

(4.50)

\displaystyle\pounds_{\underline{X}}\big((\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta})\cdot\underline{\omega}\big)=\mathsf{ad}_{\underline{X}}^{\underline{\sigma}_{1}}(\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta})\cdot\underline{\omega}+(\underline{Y}+\underline{\eta})\cdot\pounds_{\underline{X}}\underline{\omega}\ .

Definition 4.51
.

Let
V_{1}^{+}
be a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised metric on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
with isometry algebra
\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}}
. Adapted splittings
\sigma_{i}:TM\to E
with respect to
K_{i}
for
i=1,2
are
compatible
if they satisfy

(4.52)

\displaystyle\llbracket\sigma_{2}(X),e\rrbracket=\mathsf{ad}^{\sigma_{1}}_{X}\,e\ ,

for every
X\in\mathfrak{k}_{2}\subset{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(TM)
and
e\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(E)
, where

(4.53)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{2}\coloneqq\varpi_{1*}^{-1}(\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}})\cap\mathsf{\Gamma}\big(\rho(K_{2})\big)\ .

We can now state the main result proven in
[
21
]
. It provides global conditions under which a generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
can be transported to a unique generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
on
\underline{E}{}_{2}
such that the T-duality relation
R
is a generalised isometry.

Theorem 4.54
.

Let
E
be an exact Courant algebroid over
M
with isotropic subbundles
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
of the same rank such that
K_{1}^{\perp}
and
K_{2}^{\perp}
are pointwise spanned by basic sections, giving T-duality related exact Courant algebroids
\underline{E}{}_{1}
and
\underline{E}{}_{2}
with T-duality relation
R
. Assume further that the involutivity condition

(4.55)

\displaystyle\big[\mathsf{\Gamma}\bigl(\ker(\varpi_{1*})\bigr),\mathsf{\Gamma}\big(\ker(\varpi_{2*})\big)\big]\ \subseteq\ \mathsf{\Gamma}\bigl(\ker(\varpi_{1*})\bigr)+\mathsf{\Gamma}\bigl(\ker(\varpi_{2*})\bigr)

for bisubmersions holds. Let
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
be compatible adapted splittings. Let
\underline{E}{}_{1}
be endowed with a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

K_{2}^{\perp}\cap K_{1}\subseteq K_{2}\ .

(ii)

K_{2}\cap K_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq K_{1}\ .

(iii)

There exists a unique generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
on
\underline{E}{}_{2}
such that
R
is a generalised isometry between
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
, i.e.
(\underline{E}{}_{1},V_{1}^{+})
and
(\underline{E}{}_{2},V_{2}^{+})
are geometrically T-dual.

The condition (
4.55
) implies that there are unique foliations
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, whose tangent distribution is
D_{1}
, and
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}
of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
which are Hausdorff-Morita equivalent, see
[
27
]
.

Remark 4.56
.

Consider the setting of Theorem
4.54
with the isomorphism

(4.57)

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}=\sigma_{1}\oplus\tfrac{1}{2}\,\rho^{*}\colon TM\oplus T^{*}M\longrightarrow E\ .

Recall that the difference between two isotropic splittings
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
is given by

(\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2})(X)=\rho^{*}(\iota_{X}B)\ ,

for some
B\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{2}(M)
and for all
X\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(TM)
. Since
\sigma_{i}
is adapted to
K_{i}
, it then follows that

(4.58)

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}^{-1}(K_{1})=T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Psi_{1}^{-1}(K_{2})={\,\rm e}\,^{-B}\,(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\ .

Thus if
V_{1}^{+}
is a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised metric on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
, then a T-dual generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
on
\underline{E}{}_{2}
exists if
\pounds_{X}B=0
, for all
X\in\mathfrak{k}_{2}
, and
T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\subseteq\ker(B)

[
21
, Theorem 5.44]
.

The classic example of the construction of Theorem
4.54
is given by the Cavalcanti-Gualtieri formulation of T-duality
[
14
]
, see
[
21
, Section 6.1]
.

Since we have Dirac relations at our disposal, we can now discuss T-duality for Dirac structures. For this, as in Definition
4.44
, we need

Definition 4.59
.

Let
\underline{\sigma}_{1}\colon T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to\underline{E}{}_{1}
be the splitting of the exact Courant algebroid
\underline{E}{}_{1}
over
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
induced by an isotropic splitting
\sigma_{1}\colon TM\to E
of
E
which is adapted to
K_{1}\subset E
.
A Dirac structure
\underline{L}{}_{1}
on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
is
invariant with respect to
D_{1}
, or simply
D_{1}
-invariant
, if there are generators
\set{\underline{X}{}_{1},\dots,\underline{X}{}_{r_{1}}}\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
of the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
-submodule
\mathsf{\Gamma}(D_{1})
such that

(4.60)

\displaystyle\mathsf{ad}^{\underline{\sigma}_{1}}_{\underline{X}{}_{k}}(\underline{e}{}_{1})\ \in\ \mathsf{\Gamma}(\underline{L}{}_{1})\ ,

for
k=1,\dots,r_{1}
and
\underline{e}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\underline{L}{}_{1})
.

Then we arrive at a result for Dirac structures analogous to Theorem
4.54
given by

Proposition 4.61
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.54
, let
\underline{L}{}_{1}
be a
D_{1}
-invariant Dirac structure on
\underline{E}{}_{1}
. Then there exists a unique Dirac structure
\underline{L}{}_{2}
on
\underline{E}{}_{2}
such that the T-duality relation
R
is a Dirac relation.

Proof.

The construction of the T-dual Dirac structure
\underline{L}{}_{2}
on
\underline{E}{}_{2}
follows the same steps as in the construction of the T-dual generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
of Theorem
4.54
, see the proof of
[
21
, Theorem 5.27]
for details.
∎

In this setting the T-duality relation
R
restricts to a Lie algebroid relation as in Remark
4.18
.

5.
Spinors and Courant Algebroid Relations

We shall now show how to extend the notion of Courant algebroid relations to the spinor modules between related Courant algebroids, using the ideas of Section
2.3
. When applied to the instance of geometric T-duality relations, we demonstrate how our approach offers a novel alternative perspective to the Fourier-Mukai transform as well as on the T-duality invariance of Type II supergravity.

5.1.
Clifford Algebra Relations and Dirac Generating Operators

Let
E_{1}\to M_{1}
and
E_{2}\to M_{2}
be split signature pseudo-Euclidean vector bundles. For
i=1,2
, let
{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{i}}
be the spinor module over the Clifford algebra bundle
\mathsf{Cl}(E_{i})
.

Let
R
be an isotropic subbundle of
E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
supported on
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
such that
\rho(R^{\perp})\subseteq TC
. As in Definition
2.28
, we can define the space
R^{\times}
as

(5.1)

\displaystyle R^{\times}\coloneqq(R/{\rm K}_{R})/\underline{\rm CK}_{R}\ ,

where
\underline{{\rm CK}}_{R}\coloneqq(0_{E_{1}}\times E_{2})\cap(R/{\rm K}_{R})

while the kernel and cokernel of
R
are defined as in Definition
4.6
.

Definition 5.2
.

A vector subbundle
{\sf S}_{R}\subset\bigl({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}\bigr)\big\rvert_{C}
is an
R
-Clifford relation of parity
i\in\mathbbm{Z}_{2}
if it is a spinor module of the typical fibre of
\mathsf{Cl}^{i}(R)
, where the Clifford action is defined as in (
2.19
) with the fibres of the bundle
\big(\mathsf{Cl}(E_{1})\times\mathsf{Cl}(E_{2})\big)\big\rvert_{C}
acting on the fibres of
\big({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}\big)\big\rvert_{C}
.

If there exists an action of
\mathsf{Cl}(R^{\times})
on
\bigl({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}\bigr)\big\rvert_{C}
compatible with
\mathsf{Cl}^{i}(R)
and a subbundle
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\subset\bigl({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}\bigr)\big\rvert_{C}
such that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}^{i}(R^{\times})
, then
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
weak
R
-Clifford relation of parity
i\in\mathbbm{Z}_{2}
.

Proposition
2.22
and Corollary
2.25
have their smoothly varying counterparts in this case, given respectively by

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\mathtt{p}_{i}
be the projection from
E_{1}\times E_{2}
to the
i
-th factor for
i=1,2
, and denote by the same symbols the projections from
{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}
. Let
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
be an
R
-Clifford relation of parity
j
, with
\mathtt{p}_{i}(R)\neq\set{0}
and
\mathtt{p}_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})\neq\set{0}
for
i=1,2
. Then for each
c\in C

(5.4)

\displaystyle({\mathsf{S}}_{R})_{c}\cap\big(\set{0}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}\big)_{c}=({\mathsf{S}}_{R})_{c}\cap\big({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times\set{0}\big)_{c}=\set{(0,0)_{c}}\ .

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
be an
R
-Clifford relation with
\mathtt{p}_{i}(R)\neq\set{0}
and
\mathtt{p}_{i}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})\neq\set{0}
for
i=1,2
. If
(\nu,\mu)_{c},(\nu,\mu^{\prime})_{c}\in({\mathsf{S}}_{R})_{c}
for every
c\in C
, then
\mu=\mu^{\prime}
.

Example 5.6
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold with smooth leaf space
{\mathcal{Q}}
. Let

(5.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q({\mathcal{F}}):\mathbb{T}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}}

be the reduction relation of Example
4.8
with
K=T{\mathcal{F}}\oplus\set{0}
(cf. Example
3.57
), covering the surjective submersion
\varpi\colon M\to{\mathcal{Q}}
. Consider the subbundle

(5.8)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}=\set{(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},\underline{\alpha})\ |\ \underline{\alpha}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}}

supported on
\text{gr}(\varpi)
.
Recalling that
\mathrm{CK}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}=0_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
, for each
\underline{X}+\underline{\eta}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}})
there is a section
X+\eta\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathbb{T}M)
such that
(X+\eta,\underline{X}+\underline{\eta})\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\mathbb{T}M\times\overline{\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}}\,;Q({\mathcal{F}}))
, where
\eta=\varpi^{*}\underline{\eta}
and
X
is any lift of
\underline{X}
, i.e.
\varpi_{*}X=\underline{X}
. Then on
\text{gr}(\varpi)

(5.9)

\displaystyle(X+\eta,\underline{X}+\underline{\eta})\cdot(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\big(\iota_{X}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})+\eta\wedge\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\eta}\wedge\underline{\alpha}\big)

(5.10)

\displaystyle=\big(\varpi^{*}(\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\eta}\wedge\underline{\alpha}),\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\eta}\wedge\underline{\alpha}\big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\ .

Extending to the whole Clifford bundle
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
, it follows that
{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
is invariant under the action of
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
, providing it a spin representation. To show that it is an irreducible representation, suppose that
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
is invariant. Then
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
has the form

(5.11)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}=\set{(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},\underline{\alpha})\ |\ \underline{\alpha}\in S}

where
S\subseteq\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
is a vector subbundle. By invariance of
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
and projection to the second factor, it follows that
S
is invariant under the action of
\mathsf{Cl}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}})
. But
{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})
is a spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}})
, and hence
S=\set{0}
or
S=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
. Thus
{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
is a
Q({\mathcal{F}})
-Clifford relation of parity
+
.

Example 5.12
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold with compact oriented fibres and smooth leaf space
{\mathcal{Q}}
. Since the leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
are compact and oriented, there is a pushforward map

(5.13)

\displaystyle\varpi_{!}\colon{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}(M)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})\ ,\qquad\xi\longmapsto\int_{{\mathcal{F}}}\,\xi

given by the fibre integration of forms over the leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
, inducing a vector bundle morphism
\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M\to\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
denoted by the same symbol. Consider the graph
\text{gr}(\varpi_{!})
as a subspace of
(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M\times\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}})|_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}
. This is not a spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
. Indeed take any
\underline{\alpha}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
and note that
\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})=0
, thus
(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},0)\in\text{gr}(\varpi_{!})
. By Proposition
5.3
, it follows that
\text{gr}(\varpi_{!})
is not a spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
.

Consider instead a fibre volume form
\mu\in\det(T^{*}{\mathcal{F}})
and set

{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}\coloneqq\text{gr}\big(\varpi_{!}\big|_{A_{\mu}}\big)\ ,

where
A_{\mu}
is the subspace of forms
\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu
for
\underline{\alpha}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
, supported on
\text{gr}(\varpi)
. This is not an invariant subspace for the action of
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
, since if
X\in T{\mathcal{F}}={\rm ker}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
then

(X,0)\cdot\big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu,\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu)\big)=(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\iota_{X}\mu,0)\ \notin\ {\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}\ .

However, we may define an action of
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}})^{\times})
on
\bigl(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M\times\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}\bigr)\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}
. Note that
Q({\mathcal{F}})^{\times}
is the set of pairs
(X+\xi,\underline{X}+\underline{\xi})
where
X\in TM/T{\mathcal{F}}
and
\xi\in{\rm Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}})
, while
\underline{X}
and
\underline{\xi}
are canonically associated to
X
and
\xi
respectively. By considering
D={\rm Ann}(\mu)\simeq TM/T{\mathcal{F}}
, we then obtain
X_{D}\in TM
associated to
X
, and hence we define

(5.14)

\displaystyle(X+\xi,\underline{X}+\underline{\xi})\cdot\big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu,\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu)\big)\coloneqq(X_{D}+\xi,\underline{X}+\underline{\xi})\cdot\big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu,\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu)\big)

for the usual action of
\mathsf{Cl}^{j}(Q({\mathcal{F}}))
on
\bigl(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M\times\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}\bigr)\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}
, where
j={\rm dim}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathcal{F}})\mod 2
. Since
\varpi_{*}X_{D}=\underline{X}
and
\xi=\varpi^{*}\underline{\xi}
, we then obtain

(5.15)

\displaystyle(X+\xi,\underline{X}+\underline{\xi})\cdot\big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu,\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu)\big)

(5.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\big(\varpi^{*}(\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\xi}\wedge\underline{\alpha})\wedge\mu,(-1)^{j}\,(\underline{X}+\underline{\xi})\cdot\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu)\big)

(5.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt=\big(\varpi^{*}(\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\xi}\wedge\underline{\alpha})\wedge\mu,\varpi_{!}(\varpi^{*}(\iota_{\underline{X}}\underline{\alpha}+\underline{\xi}\wedge\underline{\alpha})\wedge\mu)\big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}\ ,

where we use Appendix
A
to pass the action through the fibre integration in the second factor. Thus
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}
is a weak
Q({\mathcal{F}})
-Clifford relation of parity
{\rm dim}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathcal{F}})\mod 2
.

Note that the subbundle
{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
from Example
5.6
is also a spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}})^{\times})
, and so is isomorphic to
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}
, with the isomorphism given explicitly by

(5.18)

\displaystyle(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha},\underline{\alpha})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}\longmapsto\Big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\wedge\mu\,,\,\big(\text{\footnotesize{$\int_{\mathcal{F}}$}}\,\mu\big)\ \underline{\alpha}\Big)\bigg\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}\ .

The smooth version of the canonical Clifford relation, allowing us to relate spinor lines as in Definition
2.37
, is given by

Definition 5.19
.

If
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a weak
R
-Clifford relation of parity
i
for
R\subseteq E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
, the
canonical
R
-Clifford relation of parity
i
is the vector bundle

(5.20)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{R}={\mathsf{S}}_{R}\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}

over
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
, where
n=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(E_{1})
. As a spinor module, the typical fibre of
\mathsf{Cl}^{i}(R)
acts trivially on the second component of
{\mathcal{S}}_{R}
.

If
\Omega_{1}\subset{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}
and
\Omega_{2}\subset{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}}
are pure spinor line bundles, then
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
are
R
-related
, denoted as
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
, if

(5.21)

\displaystyle(\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2})\big\rvert_{C}\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}\ \subset\ {\mathcal{S}}_{R}

is a vector subbundle.

Again the smoothly varying version of Proposition
2.43
follows as

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation supported on
C
, with spinor modules
{\mathsf{S}}_{i}
for
\mathsf{Cl}(E_{i})
for
i=1,2
and an
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\subset({\mathsf{S}}_{1}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{2})\rvert_{C}
. Let
L_{1}\subset E_{1}
and
L_{2}\subset E_{2}
be almost Dirac structures, and let
\Omega_{1}\subset{\mathsf{S}}_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}\subset{\mathsf{S}}_{2}
be the corresponding pure spinor line bundles. If
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
, then
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
.

Remark 5.23
.

The smoothly varying cases of Remarks
2.45
and
2.47
also follow. The condition that
\mathtt{p}_{1}(R)\supseteq L_{1}
or
\mathtt{p}_{2}(R)\supseteq L_{2}
is satisfied for instance when
R
is a generalised isometry, whose kernel and cokernel are necessarily trivial. It is also satisfied when
R
is the reduction relation
Q(K)
, since
\mathtt{p}_{2}(Q(K))=\underline{E}
.

Remark 5.24
.

Let us discuss Example
5.12
in relation to the work of Drummond
[
25
]
, where the reduction of pure spinors is discussed. Drummond considers a Courant algebroid reduction by an isotropic subbundle
K\subset\mathbb{T}M
. A pure spinor
\alpha
gives a representative of an almost Dirac structure
L
. The pushforward of
\alpha
is non-zero if and only if
L\cap K=\set{0}
. However, we know from Proposition
3.49
that to any almost Dirac structure
L
, such that
L\cap K^{\perp}
has constant rank, there is an associated reduced almost Dirac structure
\underline{L}
.

In the case
L\cap K\neq\set{0}
, Drummond resolves this issue by considering a perturbation
L_{D}
of
L
by a subbundle
D\subset\mathbb{T}M
. The pure spinor associated to
L_{D}
is then given by
\mu_{D}\cdot\alpha
, for some nowhere vanishing
\mu_{D}\in\det(D)
. The pure spinor
\varpi_{!}(\mu_{D}\cdot\alpha)
then generates the almost Dirac structure
\underline{L}
.

For instance, in the case that
\alpha=\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}
for some
\underline{\alpha}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\text{top}}({\mathcal{Q}})
, the corresponding Dirac structure is

L=T{\mathcal{F}}\oplus{\rm Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}})\ .

Then
D=T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
and so
\alpha_{D}=\mu\wedge\alpha
for some
\mu\in\det(T^{*}{\mathcal{F}})
. Thus the pushforward of
\alpha_{D}
by
\varpi_{!}
is non-zero.

Applying this construction to all
\underline{\alpha}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})
, the almost Dirac structure
L
changes but we may still use the same
D=T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
. One then obtains the subspace
{\mathsf{S}}_{D}\subset{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}(M)\times{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})
of pairs

(5.25)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}_{D}=\set{\Big(\alpha_{D}\,,\,\big(\text{\footnotesize{$\int_{\mathcal{F}}$}}\,\mu\big)\ \underline{\alpha}\Big)\ \Big|\ \underline{\alpha}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})}

and hence
{\mathsf{S}}_{D}={\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}
, where
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}
is as in Example
5.12
and the Clifford action given by Equation (
5.14
). For an almost Dirac structure
L\subset\mathbb{T}M
,
{\mathsf{S}}_{D}
is an
L
-weak
Q({\mathcal{F}})
-Clifford relation (see Remark
2.47
) if and only if
\Omega\subset\mathtt{p}_{1}({\mathsf{S}}_{D})
for the pure spinor line bundle
\Omega
associated to
L
.

We highlight that, while Drummond perturbs the almost Dirac structure
L
to
L_{D}
to obtain a reduced pure spinor with line bundle
\underline{\Omega}
such that
\Omega\sim_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\underline{\Omega}
, this can also be achieved by considering instead an isomorphic spinor module for
\mathsf{Cl}(Q({\mathcal{F}})^{\times})
. For instance, the case
L\cap K=\set{0}
requires the spinor module
{\mathsf{S}}_{D}
, while the case
K\subset L
can be considered using the spinor module
{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
as in Example
5.6
.

Definition 5.26
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a foliation of
M
.

(a)

A form
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}(M)
is
invariant along
{\mathcal{F}}
or
T{\mathcal{F}}
-invariant
if there is a Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{F}})
spanning
T{\mathcal{F}}
pointwise such that
\pounds_{X}\omega=0
for each
X\in\mathfrak{f}
.

(b)

A pure spinor line bundle
\Omega\subset\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M
is
invariant along
{\mathcal{F}}
or
T{\mathcal{F}}
-invariant
if there is a Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{F}})
spanning
T{\mathcal{F}}
pointwise such that
\pounds_{X}\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega)\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega)
for each
X\in\mathfrak{f}
.

If
\omega
is invariant along
{\mathcal{F}}
then the line bundle
\Omega^{\omega}
generated by
\omega
is invariant along
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Example 5.27
.

Let
\underline{\alpha}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}})
be a nowhere vanishing form on
{\mathcal{Q}}=M/{\mathcal{F}}
, and consider the pure spinor line bundle
\Omega\subset\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}M
generated by
\alpha=\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}
. Then
\Omega
is invariant along
{\mathcal{F}}
, since for
X\in T{\mathcal{F}}
and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
,

(5.28)

\displaystyle\pounds_{X}(f\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\iota_{X}\,\mathrm{d}(f\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})+\mathrm{d}(f\,\iota_{X}\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\iota_{X}(\mathrm{d}f\wedge\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha})+f\,\iota_{X}\varpi^{*}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\alpha})+0

\displaystyle=(\pounds_{X}f)\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\alpha}\ \in\ \Omega\ .

For the
Q({\mathcal{F}})
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}
of Example
5.6
, denote by
\underline{\Omega}
the pure spinor line bundle generated by
\underline{\alpha}
. For each
u\in(\Omega\times\underline{\Omega})\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}
and
c=(m,\varpi(m))
with
m\in M
,
we find

(5.29)

\displaystyle u_{c}=(f\,\alpha,\underline{g}\,\underline{\alpha})_{c}=\big(f(m)\,\alpha_{m},\underline{g}(\varpi(m))\,\underline{\alpha}_{\varpi(m)}\big)=\big(f(m)\,\alpha_{m},\tfrac{\underline{g}(\varpi(m))}{f(m)}\,f(m)\,\underline{\alpha}_{\varpi(m)}\big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})c}^{\kappa(c)}

where
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
and
\underline{g}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}})
are nowhere vanishing functions, while
\kappa\in C^{\infty}(\text{gr}(\varpi))
is given by

\kappa(c)=\frac{\varpi^{*}\underline{g}(m)}{f(m)}\ .

Thus

(5.30)

\displaystyle(\Omega\times\underline{\Omega})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}

\displaystyle=(\Omega\times\underline{\Omega})\big\rvert_{\text{gr}(\varpi)}\otimes|\det({\mathsf{S}}^{\kappa}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}

\displaystyle\subset\ {\mathsf{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}^{\kappa}\otimes|\det({\mathsf{S}}^{\kappa}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\ ,

where
n=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}M
. Hence
\Omega\sim_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\underline{\Omega}
.

5.1.1.
Relating Dirac Generating Operators

Let
R\subset E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
be an isotropic subbundle supported on
C\subset M_{1}\times M_{2}
such that
\rho(R^{\perp})\subseteq TC.
Let
{\mathsf{D}}_{i}
be a Dirac generating operator (DGO) on the spinor module
{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{i}}
over
\mathsf{Cl}(E_{i})
, for
i=1,2
. Suppose that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\subset({\mathsf{S}}_{E_{1}}\times{\mathsf{S}}_{E_{2}})\rvert_{C}
is an
R
-Clifford relation of parity
j
.

Definition 5.31
.

{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
({\mathsf{D}}_{1},{\mathsf{D}}_{2})
-DGO relation
if

{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is invariant under
{\mathsf{D}}_{1}\times(-1)^{j}\,{\mathsf{D}}_{2}
.

This notion yields a Courant algebroid relation for structures defined as in Theorem
3.14
through

Proposition 5.32
.

Let
R\subset E_{1}\times\overline{E}_{2}
be a Lagrangian subbundle supported on
C\subset M_{1}\times M_{2}
with trivial kernel and cokernel. Suppose that
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
has a faithful irreducible spinor representation
{\mathsf{S}}
, and that
{\mathsf{S}}
is a
({\mathsf{D}}_{1},{\mathsf{D}}_{2})
-DGO relation. Then
R
is a Courant algebroid relation.

Proof.

According to Theorem
3.14
,

(5.33)

\displaystyle\llbracket r,r^{\prime}\rrbracket\cdot{\mathsf{s}}\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}\ ,

for every
s\in{\mathsf{S}}
and
r=(e_{1},e_{2}),r^{\prime}=(e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(R)
. Let
c\in C
.
Since
\ker(R)=\set{0}
, there is some
{}^{R}\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1}
such that
(\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1},^{R}\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1})\in R_{c}
. Then
(\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1},^{R}\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1})\cdot{\mathsf{s}}\in{\mathsf{S}}_{c}
, and it follows that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\big(0\cdot{\mathsf{s}}_{1},(^{R}\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1}-\llbracket e_{2},e_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{2})\cdot{\mathsf{s}}_{2}\big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{c}\ ,

for every
{\mathsf{s}}=({\mathsf{s}}_{1},{\mathsf{s}}_{2})\in{\mathsf{S}}_{c}
.
By Proposition
5.3
, the second factor must vanish.
Since
{\mathsf{S}}
is a faithful representation,
{}^{R}\llbracket e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1}=\llbracket e_{2},e_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{2}
. Since this happens for all
c\in C
, it follows that
R
is involutive and thus defines a Courant algebroid relation.
∎

5.2.
Integrability of Almost Dirac Structures

Let
L
be an almost Dirac structure of a twisted standard Courant algebroid
(\mathbb{T}M,H)
, and let
\Omega
be the corresponding pure spinor line bundle. By
[
29
, Proposition 3.44]
,
L
is a Dirac structure (i.e. it is integrable) if and only if the Dirac generating operator
\mathrm{d}_{H}
satisfies
\mathrm{d}_{H}(\Omega)\subset L^{*}\cdot\Omega\simeq L^{*}\otimes\Omega
, where
L^{*}\simeq\mathbb{T}M/L
. That is, for any nowhere vanishing section
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\Omega)
, there is a section
X+\xi
of
\mathbb{T}M
such that

(5.35)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}\omega=(X+\xi)\cdot\omega\ .

Suppose
R\colon(\mathbb{T}M_{1},H_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}M_{2},H_{2})
is a Courant algebroid relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
of parity
j
, and assume that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
(\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}})
-DGO relation. Suppose that
L_{i}
is an almost Dirac structure on
\mathbb{T}M_{i}
with pure spinor line bundle
\Omega_{i}
, for
i=1,2
, and that
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
. Then
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
by Proposition
5.22
.

If
L_{1}
is integrable, then for every
\omega_{1}\in\Omega_{1}
there is a section
X_{1}+\xi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathbb{T}M_{1})
such that

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}}\omega_{1}=(X_{1}+\xi_{1})\cdot\omega_{1}\ .

If
\mathtt{p}_{1}(R)\supseteq L_{1}
, then there is a section
X_{2}+\xi_{2}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\mathbb{T}M_{2})
such that
X_{1}+\xi_{1}\sim_{R}X_{2}+\xi_{2}
. Let
\omega_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
with
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
. Since
\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}}\omega_{1}\sim_{R}(-1)^{j}\,\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}}\omega_{2}
and
(X_{1}+\xi_{1})\cdot\omega_{1}\sim_{R}(-1)^{j}\,(X_{2}+\xi_{2})\cdot\omega_{2}
, by Corollary
5.5
it follows that

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}}\omega_{2}=(X_{2}+\xi_{2})\cdot\omega_{2}

and hence
L_{2}
is integrable. Thus we have shown

Proposition 5.38
.

Let
R\colon(\mathbb{T}M_{1},H_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}M_{2},H_{2})
be a Courant algebroid relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
that is a
(\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}})
-DGO relation. Take an almost Dirac structure
L_{i}
on
\mathbb{T}M_{i}
with associated pure spinor line bundle
\Omega_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Suppose that
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
and that
\mathtt{p}_{i}(R)\supseteq L_{i}
. Then
L_{1}
is integrable if and only if
L_{2}
is integrable.

5.3.
Spinor Relations for Geometric T-Duality

In the setting of geometric T-duality with
K_{1}\cap K_{2}=\set{0}
, we may transport pure spinor line bundles to the T-dual manifold through

Theorem 5.39
.

Let
R\colon\mathbb{T}\mathcal{Q}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{T}\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be a geometric T-duality relation supported on
\underline{C}\subset{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. If
\Omega_{1}\subset\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
is a
D_{1}
-invariant pure spinor line bundle, then there is a pure spinor line bundle
\Omega_{2}\subset\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
such that
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\psi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\Omega_{1})
be a section of
\Omega_{1}
. Then by Equation (
4.50
),

(5.40)

\displaystyle 0=\pounds_{\underline{X}}(\underline{e}\cdot\psi_{1})=\mathsf{ad}^{\underline{\sigma}_{1}}_{\underline{X}}(\underline{e})\cdot\psi_{1}+\underline{e}\cdot\pounds_{\underline{X}}\psi_{1}\ ,

for
\underline{e}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}})
and
\underline{X}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
. It follows that
\Omega_{1}
is
D_{1}
-invariant if and only if the null Lagrangian subbundle
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}}
is
D_{1}
-invariant, i.e.
\mathsf{ad}^{\underline{\sigma}_{1}}_{\underline{X}}\,\mathsf{\Gamma}(\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}})\subset\mathsf{\Gamma}(\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}})
for each
\underline{X}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(D_{1})
. According to
[
22
, Lemma 3.32]
, we may thus take a spanning set of
D_{1}
-invariant sections for
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}}
which can be carried to
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
, and define
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{2}}
as the span of these sections. It follows that
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{2}}
is Lagrangian and
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{1}}\sim_{R}\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{2}}
. There is a pure spinor line bundle
\Omega_{2}
associated with
\underline{N}{}_{\psi_{2}}
.
By Remark
5.23
, at each point
c=(c_{1},c_{2})\in\underline{C}
it follows that
\Omega_{1}{}_{c_{1}}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}{}_{c_{2}}
. Since this is true for all
c\in\underline{C}
, it follows that
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
.
∎

5.3.1.
Twisted Differentials and the Fourier-Mukai Transform

We shall now show a converse to Proposition
5.32
. By this we mean to show how, in this setting, the geometric T-duality relation gives rise to a DGO relation. To do this, we will restrict to the case that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are both fibre bundles over a manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
, with bundle projections
\pi_{i}:{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{B}}
, and

(5.41)

\displaystyle M={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\ \subset\ {\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}

is the correspondence space with the canonical projections
\varpi_{i}=\mathtt{p}_{i}:M\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Then the support of the T-duality relation

(5.42)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R:(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})}

is
\underline{C}=M
.
We will construct a spinor module
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
for
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
, and subsequently construct the canonical Dirac generating operator for this spinor module (cf. Theorem
3.17
), following the constructions of
[
17
]
. We will then show that this Dirac generating operator is in fact given by the pair of twisted differentials
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
, extended from
{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\times{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
to
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
as graded derivations.

The construction of
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is by means of a Courant algebroid
R_{\mathcal{B}}
over
{\mathcal{B}}
whose typical fibre is isomorphic to a fibre of
R
. In the following we denote
\underline{E}{}_{i}=\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
and abbreviate subscripts
{}_{\underline{E}{}_{i}}
simply by
i
, for
i=1,2.

Proposition 5.43
.

There is a transitive Courant algebroid
R_{\mathcal{B}}
over
{\mathcal{B}}
which is fibrewise isomorphic (as a vector bundle) to
R
.

Proof.

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are both fibre bundles over the base manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
, fitting the commutative diagram

(5.44)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

Then
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\underline{E}{}_{1}\times\overline{\underline{E}}_{2};R)
is a locally-free finitely-generated
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module, where the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module structure is given by pullback
\pi_{1}^{*}\times\pi_{2}^{*}:C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\to C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
. Hence by the smooth version of the Serre-Swan Theorem (as formulated in
[
44
, Chapter 11]
) there exists a vector bundle
R_{\mathcal{B}}
over
{\mathcal{B}}
whose space of sections coincides with
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\underline{E}{}_{1}\times\overline{\underline{E}}_{2};R)
.

The bundle
R_{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
inherits the structure of a Courant algebroid in the following way. Let
c=(c_{1},c_{2})\in\underline{C}
, which has a corresponding point
b=\pi_{1}(\varpi_{1}(c_{1}))=\pi_{2}(\varpi_{2}(c_{2}))\in{\mathcal{B}}
. We denote a point of the fibre
R_{\mathcal{B}}{}_{b}
by the same symbol as the corresponding point
(\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{2})\in R_{c}
, where the context will be clear. By the construction of
R
, this is independent of the point
c\in\underline{C}
corresponding to
b\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

Then the anchor
\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}:R_{\mathcal{B}}\to T{\mathcal{B}}
is given by

(5.45)

\displaystyle\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}(\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{2})_{b}\coloneqq\pi_{1}{}_{*}\,\underline{\rho}{}_{1}(\underline{e}_{1})_{c_{1}}=\pi_{2}{}_{*}\,\underline{\rho}{}_{2}(\underline{e}_{1})_{c_{2}}\ ,

which is well-defined since

\rho(\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{2})=\big(\underline{\rho}{}_{1}(\underline{e}_{1}),\underline{\rho}{}_{2}(\underline{e}_{2})\big)\ \in\ T\underline{C}

projects to the same vector in
T{\mathcal{B}}
under pushforward by
\pi_{1}{}_{*}\circ\varpi_{1*}
and
\pi_{2}{}_{*}\circ\varpi_{2*}
. Similarly, the pairing is given by

(5.46)

\displaystyle\big\langle(\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{2})\,,\,(\underline{e}^{\prime}_{1},\underline{e}^{\prime}_{2})\big\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}(b):=\langle\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle_{1}(c_{1})=\langle\underline{e}_{2},\underline{e}_{2}^{\prime}\rangle_{2}(c_{2})\ ,

while the Dorfman bracket is

(5.47)

\displaystyle\llbracket(\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{2})\,,\,(\underline{e}^{\prime}_{1},\underline{e}^{\prime}_{2})\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}{}_{b}}:=\big(\llbracket\underline{e}_{1},\underline{e}_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{1c_{1}}\,,\,\llbracket\underline{e}_{2},\underline{e}_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{2c_{2}}\big)\ ,

where we used the usual Courant algebroid structures on
R\subset\underline{E}{}_{1}\times\overline{\underline{E}}_{2}
.
∎

By a result of Chen-Stiénon-Xu
[
15
, Lemma 1.2]
, a regular Courant algebroid
E
admits isomorphisms

(5.48)

\displaystyle E\simeq\mathrm{im}(\rho_{E})\oplus\ker(\rho_{E})\simeq\mathrm{im}(\rho_{E})\oplus{\mathscr{G}}\oplus\ker(\rho_{E})^{\perp}\ ,

such that
\mathrm{im}(\rho_{E})
is isotropic in
E
and
{\mathscr{G}}\simeq\ker(\rho_{E})/\ker(\rho_{E})^{\perp}
is orthogonal to
\mathrm{im}(\rho_{E})
in
E
. In this splitting, the restrictions of the Dorfman bracket and pairing of
E
make
{\mathscr{G}}
into a bundle of quadratic Lie algebras.

For the transitive Courant algebroid
R_{\mathcal{B}}
constructed in Proposition
5.43
, this provides the splitting

(5.49)

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{B}}\simeq T{\mathcal{B}}\oplus{\mathscr{G}}\oplus T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\ .

The choice

{\mathscr{G}}\simeq\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})\,\big/\,\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\perp}\ \subset\ \mathrm{im}(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\perp}=T{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}

is canonically associated to
R_{\mathcal{B}}
, and the projection
\mathtt{p}_{\mathscr{G}}
to
{\mathscr{G}}
is calculated from the relation
R\subset\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
: for
g\in{\mathscr{G}}
we choose a lift
\widehat{g}=(\dot{g},\ddot{g})\in\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})\subset R
. Note that
\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})\subset T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}\times T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}
in this lift to
R
. The choice of splitting (
5.49
) is then equivalent to the unique lifts of
X\in T{\mathcal{B}}
to
\dot{X}\in T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
\ddot{X}\in T{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
such that
\widehat{X}\coloneqq(\dot{X},\ddot{X})\in R
, while for
\omega\in T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
we set
\widehat{\omega}\coloneqq(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega)\in R
.

The Courant algebroid structures in the splitting (
5.49
) are then given by

(5.50)

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{X}+\widehat{g}+\widehat{\omega},\widehat{Y}+\widehat{g}^{\prime}+\widehat{\chi}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\,(\iota_{X}\omega+\iota_{Y}\chi)+\langle\widehat{g},\widehat{g}^{\prime}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ ,

(5.51)

\displaystyle\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}(\widehat{X}+\widehat{g}+\widehat{\omega})

\displaystyle=X\ ,

(5.52)

\displaystyle\llbracket\widehat{X}+\widehat{g}+\widehat{\omega},\widehat{Y}+\widehat{g}^{\prime}+\widehat{\chi}\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}

\displaystyle=\big(\llbracket\dot{X}+\dot{g}+\pi_{1}^{*}\omega,\dot{Y}+\dot{g}^{\prime}+\pi_{1}^{*}\chi\rrbracket_{1}\,,\,\llbracket\ddot{X}+\ddot{g}+\pi_{2}^{*}\omega,\ddot{Y}+\ddot{g}^{\prime}+\pi_{2}^{*}\chi\rrbracket_{2}\big)\ .

One easily checks that these operations are well-defined.

By results of Alekseev-Xu
[
4
, Theorem 4.1]
, if
E
is a regular Courant algebroid then every spinor bundle
{\mathsf{S}}
over
\mathsf{Cl}(E)
locally admits a Dirac generating operator which can be expressed by means of a Courant algebroid connection, as we discuss below. On the weighted spinor bundle there is a canonical Dirac generating operator
[
17
, Theorem 52]
. For illustration, let us first discuss how this works in the case of principal circle bundles.

Example 5.53

(
Circle Bundles
)
.

Let us consider the T-duality relation
R
when
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are circle bundles over
{\mathcal{B}}
, see
[
21
, Section 6]
and
[
22
, Example 2.42]
. The restriction of the Courant algebroid
R_{\mathcal{B}}
to any open subset
U\subset{\mathcal{B}}
is given by

(5.54)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(U,R_{\mathcal{B}})=\set{(X,X)}{X\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(TU)}\,\oplus\,\set{(\xi,\xi)}{\xi\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T^{*}U)}\,\oplus\,\text{Span}_{C^{\infty}(U)}\set{(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})\,,\,(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})}

for a choice of connections
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
respectively, pulled back to
{\mathcal{B}}
and restricted to
U
. The vector fields
\partial_{\theta_{i}}
are dual to
\theta_{i}
for
i=1,2.

Thus

(5.55)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(U,\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})\big)

\displaystyle=\set{(\xi,\xi)}{\xi\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T^{*}U)}\,\oplus\,\text{Span}_{C^{\infty}(U)}\set{(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})\,,\,(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})}\ ,

(5.56)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(U,\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle=\set{(\xi,\xi)}{\xi\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T^{*}U)}\ .

It follows that the restriction of the bundle
{\mathscr{G}}\simeq\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})/\ker(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\perp}
to
U
is given by

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(U,{\mathscr{G}})=\text{Span}_{C^{\infty}(U)}\set{(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})\,,\,(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})}

and

(5.57)

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{B}}\big\rvert_{U}\simeq TU\oplus T^{*}U\oplus{\mathscr{G}}\big\rvert_{U}\ .

In the following we drop the explicit restriction to
U\subset{\mathcal{B}}
from the notation for brevity.

In order to define the bracket of two sections of
\mathrm{im}(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})=T{\mathcal{B}}
, we note that for circle bundles (and more generally principal bundles) a choice of lift of
X\in T{\mathcal{B}}
to
(\dot{X},\ddot{X})\in R
is equivalent to the choice of connections
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
), and the lifts are thus the horizontal lifts
(X^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},X^{\mathrm{h}_{2}})\in T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times T{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
with respect to these connections. The requirement that
(X^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},X^{\mathrm{h}_{2}})\in R
is the condition

\underline{H}{}_{1}-\underline{H}{}_{2}=\mathrm{d}(\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2})\ ,

as discussed in
[
14
]
.

The twisting three-forms are given by
[
14
]

\underline{H}{}_{i}=\pi_{i}^{*}h+c_{3-i}\wedge\theta_{i}\ ,

for
i=1,2
, where
c_{j}=\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}
represents the Chern class of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}\to{\mathcal{B}}
for
j=1,2
and
h\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{3}({\mathcal{B}})
.
The bracket of two elements of
\mathrm{im}(\rho_{R_{\mathcal{B}}})=T{\mathcal{B}}
is then

(5.58)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \llbracket(X,X)\,,\,(Y,Y)\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}&=\big([X,Y]\,,\,[X,Y]\big)+\mathtt{p}_{T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}}\big(\underline{H}{}_{1}(X^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},\,\cdot\,)\,,\,\underline{H}{}_{2}(X^{{\mathrm{h}_{2}}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{2}},\,\cdot\,)\big)\\
&\qquad\,+\mathtt{p}_{\mathscr{G}}\big([X^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{1}}]+\underline{H}{}_{1}(X^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{1}},\,\cdot\,)\,,\,[X^{\mathrm{h}_{2}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{2}}]+\underline{H}{}_{2}(X^{\mathrm{h}_{2}},Y^{\mathrm{h}_{2}},\,\cdot\,)\big)\\[4.0pt]
&=\big([X,Y]\,,\,[X,Y]\big)+\big(h(X,Y,\,\cdot\,)\,,\,h(X,Y,\,\cdot\,))\\
&\qquad\,+c_{1}(X,Y)\,(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})+c_{2}(X,Y)\,(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})\ .\end{aligned}

By
[
1
, Section 1.2]
,
(1,\theta_{2})
is a pure spinor for
\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})
with Lagrangian subbundle
L\subset{\mathscr{G}}
given by
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(L)=\text{Span}_{C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})}\set{(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})}
, and thus defines a spinor bundle
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}=\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})/(\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})\cdot L)
. The subbundle
L^{*}\subset{\mathscr{G}}
transverse to
L
given by

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(L^{*})=\text{Span}_{C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})}\set{(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})}

provides an isomorphism

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}})\simeq{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}L^{*})\cdot(1,\theta_{2})=\textrm{Span}_{C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})}\set{(1,\theta_{2})\,,\,(\theta_{1},1)}\ .

The spinor bundle for
\mathsf{Cl}(R_{{\mathcal{B}}})=\mathsf{Cl}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{B}})\otimes\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})
is then

(5.59)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}_{R}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}\ .

It has
-
parity.

We define the map
\overline{W}:{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})\to{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})
by

(5.60)

\displaystyle\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})=\omega\wedge(c_{2},c_{2})\otimes(\theta_{1},\partial_{\theta_{2}})\cdot{\mathsf{s}}-\omega\wedge(c_{1},c_{1})\otimes(\partial_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{2})\cdot{\mathsf{s}}\ ,

for
\omega\in\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathcal{B}})
and
{\mathsf{s}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}})
.
As in
[
17
, Theorem 61]
, one can then define the canonical Dirac generating operator on
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
as

(5.61)
