\displaystyle{\mathsf{D}}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})=(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}-\big((h,h)\wedge\omega\big)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}+(-1)^{|\omega|+1}\,\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})\ .

After pullback by
\pi_{1}^{*}\times\pi_{2}^{*}
, this reduces to the Dirac generating operator
\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}}\times(-\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
on
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
and provides a
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
-DGO relation.

We now extend Example
5.53
to the general case where
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are fibred over the same manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
with surjective submersions
\pi_{i}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})={\mathcal{B}}
.
By Proposition
5.43
, there is a transitive Courant algebroid
R_{\mathcal{B}}
over
{\mathcal{B}}
that is fibrewise isomorphic to
R\subset\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
.
Given a splitting
R_{\mathcal{B}}\simeq\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{B}}\oplus{\mathscr{G}}
, define

{\mathscr{G}}_{1}^{\star}=\mathtt{p}_{T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}\,\mathtt{p}_{1}({\mathscr{G}})\ \subset\ T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\ .

Consider the spinor

u=(\lambda,1)

of the Clifford algebra bundle
\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})
, where
\lambda\in\det{\mathscr{G}}_{1}^{\star}
(this can always be done locally, provided that the typical fibre of
D_{1}=T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
is orientable). This is a pure spinor, with Lagrangian subbundle
L\subset{\mathscr{G}}
. Choose its dual
L^{*}
such that

\underline{\rho}{}_{2}(\mathtt{p}_{2}(L^{*}))=\set{0}\ .

This then yields a spinor bundle

{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}L^{*}\cdot u\ \subset\ \left(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\right)\big\rvert_{\underline{C}}\ .

The bundle

(5.62)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}_{R}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}

over
{\mathcal{B}}
is a spinor representation for the Clifford bundle
\mathsf{Cl}(R_{\mathcal{B}})
, with action

(5.63)

\displaystyle(\widehat{X}+\widehat{g}+\widehat{\chi})\cdot(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})=(X+\chi)\cdot\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}}+(-1)^{|\omega|}\,\omega\otimes g\cdot{\mathsf{s}}

for
\widehat{X}+\widehat{g}+\widehat{\chi}\in R_{\mathcal{B}}
,
\omega\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
, and
{\mathsf{s}}\in{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
. These actions are performed using the Clifford action (
2.19
) of
\mathsf{Cl}(R_{\mathcal{B}})
on
(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})\rvert_{\underline{C}}
by pulling
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
back by
\pi_{1}^{*}\times\pi_{2}^{*}
, where we recall that
\underline{C}\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. By construction,
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is also a spinor representation for
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
, with the same action. We leave the pullback by
\pi_{1}^{*}\times\pi_{2}^{*}
implicit in the following.

Proposition 5.64
.

{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is an
R
-Clifford relation of parity
j=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}(\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1})\mod 2
.

Proof.

By
[
17
, Remark 57]
it follows that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is an irreducible spinor representation for
\mathsf{Cl}^{j}(R_{\mathcal{B}})
, where
j=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathscr{G}})\mod 2
, and hence for
\mathsf{Cl}^{j}(R)
. In the present situation

\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathscr{G}})=2\,\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(L)=2\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}(\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1})\ ,

and the result follows.
∎

In accordance with
[
18
]
, the canonical
R
-Clifford relation we consider is

{\mathcal{S}}_{R}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{*}\,|^{\frac{1}{\tt g}}\ ,

where
\tt g
is the rank of
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
, since the bundle of forms
\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
is canonically defined over
\underline{C}
.

Example 5.65

(
The Fourier-Mukai Transform
)
.

Consider the setting of T-dual torus bundles described in
[
9
,
14
]
. Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
be principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-bundles over
{\mathcal{B}}
, where
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
is a
k
-dimensional torus, endowed with closed
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant three-forms
\underline{H}{}_{1}
and
\underline{H}{}_{2}
respectively. Then
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
is a principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-bundle over
{\mathcal{B}}
. Choose a
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-invariant two-form
B\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{2}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
whose restriction to
\ker(\varpi_{1}{}_{*})\otimes\ker(\varpi_{2}{}_{*})
is non-degenerate and which satisfies

(5.66)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}B=\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}-\varpi_{2}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{2}\ .

Let

(5.67)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{R}}:{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})

be an isomorphism of
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-modules, where the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module structure on
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{i})
is given by pullback
\pi_{i}^{*}:C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\to C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})
for
i=1,2
.

There is an isomorphism of twisted cohomology

\varrho\colon{\mathsf{H}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{H}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})\ ,

which at the level of differential forms is given by

(5.68)

\displaystyle\varrho(\omega)\coloneqq\int_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}\,{\,\rm e}\,^{B}\wedge\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\omega

for
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
, where the integration is along the fibres of the projection
\varpi_{2}:{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. The map
\varrho
satisfies the crucial properties
8
8

8

See Appendix
A
for the origin of the grading factors
(-1)^{k}
in (
5.69
).

(5.69)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}}\varrho(\omega)=(-1)^{k}\,\varrho(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}}\omega)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathscr{R}(e)\cdot\varrho(\omega)=(-1)^{k}\,\varrho(e\cdot\omega)\ ,

for
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
,
e\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and a unique choice of (
5.67
)
[
14
]
.
This implies that
\varrho
is an isomorphism between the twisted differential complexes of torus-invariant forms
({\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1}),\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}})
and
({\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2}),\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
, as well as an isomorphism of irreducible Clifford modules. This is a smooth version of the Fourier-Mukai transform.

By
[
21
, Lemma 6.5]
, there is a T-duality relation
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})
supported on
\underline{C}={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. When restricted to
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant sections it gives rise to the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module isomorphism (
5.67
) by
[
21
, Proposition 6.13]
; with a slight abuse of notation, we continue to use the same symbol
R
for this restriction. Because of the second property in (
5.69
), it follows that the graph of the Fourier-Mukai transform

\text{gr}(\varrho)\ \subset\ \big({\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\times{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})\big)\big|_{\underline{C}}

is invariant under the action of
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
defined as follows: if
c=(c_{1},c_{2})\in\underline{C}
,
(e_{1},e_{2})\in R_{c}
and
\omega\in(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}/{\mathsf{T}}^{k})_{c_{1}}
, then

(5.70)

\displaystyle(e_{1},e_{2})\cdot\big(\omega,\varrho(\omega)\big):=\big(e_{1}\cdot\omega,(-1)^{k}\,e_{2}\cdot\varrho(\omega)\big)=\big(e_{1}\cdot\omega,\varrho(e_{2}\cdot\omega)\big)\ \in\ \text{gr}(\varrho)_{c}\ ,

where
k
is the dimension of the fibres
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
and hence defines the parity of a representation of
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
.
Thus
\text{gr}(\varrho)
is a spinor representation of
\mathsf{Cl}^{(-1)^{k}}(R)
.

We show that
\text{gr}(\varrho)
is an
R
-Clifford relation of parity
k\mod 2
. Suppose that there is a
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
-invariant subbundle
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}\subset\text{gr}(\varrho)
. It follows that
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}=\text{gr}(\varrho|_{{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{1}})
for some subspace
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{1}\subset{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
. Then
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{1}
is invariant under
\mathsf{Cl}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}/{\mathsf{T}}^{k})
since
{\mathsf{S}}^{\prime}
is invariant under
\mathsf{Cl}(R)
. By the irreducibility of
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
as a
\mathsf{Cl}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}/{\mathsf{T}}^{k})
-module, it follows that either
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{1}=\set{0}
or
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{1}={\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
, and hence
\text{gr}(\varrho)
is an irreducible representation of
\mathsf{Cl}^{(-1)^{k}}(R)
.

Since an irreducible Clifford module is unique up to isomorphism, it follows that the irreducible representation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
constructed in (
5.62
) is isomorphic to
\text{gr}(\varrho)
. Thus the
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
recovers the Fourier-Mukai transform.

In order to write a Dirac generating operator on
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
, following
[
15
, Section 1.3]
we introduce the maps

(5.71)

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\times\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}({\mathscr{G}})\ ,\qquad\qquad\hskip 25.6073pt\ W(X,Y)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathscr{G}}\llbracket\widehat{X},\widehat{Y}\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ ,

(5.72)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\times\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\times\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\longrightarrow C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\ ,\quad{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y,Z)=\langle\mathtt{p}_{T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}}\llbracket\widehat{X},\widehat{Y}\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}},\widehat{Z}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ ,

(5.73)

\displaystyle\nabla

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(T{\mathcal{B}})\times\mathsf{\Gamma}({\mathscr{G}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}({\mathscr{G}})\ ,\qquad\qquad\quad\hskip 46.94687pt\nabla_{X}g=\mathtt{p}_{\mathscr{G}}\llbracket\widehat{X},\widehat{g}\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ ,

for all
X,Y,Z\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T{\mathcal{B}})
and
g\in\mathsf{\Gamma}({\mathscr{G}})
. We write
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}=(\dot{h},\ddot{h})\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{3}R)
with
h\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{3}({\mathcal{B}})
.

Let
\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}:{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T{\mathcal{B}})\times{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}})\to{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}})
be a
T{\mathcal{B}}
-connection on
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
compatible with
\nabla
, that is

(5.74)

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}_{X}(g\cdot{\mathsf{s}})=\nabla_{X}g\cdot{\mathsf{s}}+g\cdot\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}_{X}{\mathsf{s}}\ ,

for
X\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(TM)
,
g\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathscr{G}})
and
{\mathsf{s}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}).

Let
C\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{3}{\mathscr{G}}^{*})
be the three-form on
{\mathscr{G}}
given by

C(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})=\langle\llbracket\widehat{g},\widehat{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{R_{\mathcal{B}}},\widehat{g}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ ,

for
g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathscr{G}})
, viewed as a section of
\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}})
; then
C
acts by Clifford multiplication on
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
. Finally, define the map
\overline{W}:{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})\to{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{R})
as

(5.75)

\displaystyle\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j,\mu}\,\langle\hbox{\hskip 411.22394pt\hskip-24.36862pt\hbox{$W(X_{i},X_{j})$}\hskip-24.36862pt\hskip-411.22394pt\raisebox{2.54445pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.11778pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{677201}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-206.03706pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03706pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03552pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03593pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-411.22394pt\hskip 411.22394pt}\,,\,\widehat{g}_{\mu}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ \alpha_{i}\wedge\alpha_{j}\wedge\omega\otimes\tilde{g}_{\mu}\cdot{\mathsf{s}}\ ,

for
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{B}})
and
{\mathsf{s}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}({\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}})
, where
(X_{i})
is a (local) basis for
T{\mathcal{B}}
with
(\alpha_{i})
its canonical dual basis for
T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
, i.e.
\alpha_{i}(X_{j})=\iota_{X_{j}}\alpha_{i}=\delta_{ij}
, while
(g_{\mu})
is a basis for
{\mathscr{G}}
with
(\tilde{g}_{\mu})
its dual basis for
{\mathscr{G}}^{*}\simeq{\mathscr{G}}
with respect to the pairing on
{\mathscr{G}}
induced by
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}
, i.e.
\tilde{g}_{\mu}(g_{\nu})=\langle\widehat{g}_{\mu},\widehat{g}_{\nu}\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}=\delta_{\mu\nu}
.

With these ingredients, the canonical Dirac generating operator
{\mathsf{D}}
on
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is then given by
[
17
, Theorem 61]

(5.76)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathsf{D}}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})&=(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}+\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}({\mathsf{s}})\wedge\omega-({\mathcal{H}}\wedge\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}\\
&\qquad\,+(-1)^{|\omega|+1}\,\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})+\tfrac{1}{4}\,(-1)^{|\omega|+1}\,\omega\otimes C\cdot{\mathsf{s}}\ .\end{aligned}

Our main result concerning a converse to Proposition
5.32
, which can be formulated in the realm of geometric T-duality, is now formulated as

Theorem 5.77
.

Let
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})
be a geometric T-duality relation, and let
R_{\mathcal{B}}
be the transitive Courant algebroid constructed in Proposition
5.43
. Let
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
be the
R
-Clifford relation of parity
j=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}(\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1})\mod 2
defined by (
5.62
) with respect to the splitting of
R_{\mathcal{B}}
given in (
5.49
).
Let
{\mathsf{D}}
be the canonical Dirac generating operator on
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
given by (
5.76
). Choose
\lambda\in\det({\mathscr{G}}^{\star}_{1})
so that the pure spinor line bundle
\Omega^{\lambda}
generated by
\lambda
is
D_{1}
-invariant, i.e.
\pounds_{\underline{X}}\lambda\in\Omega^{\lambda}
for all
\underline{X}\in\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}}
.

Then

{\mathsf{D}}=\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}}\times(-1)^{j}\,\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}}\ .

In particular,
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
-DGO relation.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\omega}=(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega)
and
\widehat{\mathsf{s}}=(\dot{\ell},\ddot{\ell}\,)\cdot u=(\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda,\ddot{\ell}\,)
, where
u=(\lambda,1)
is
D_{1}
-invariant. In the following calculations, we omit the parity
j
of the
R
-Clifford relation for brevity.
We consider each term in Equation (
5.76
) individually: The first term simply becomes

(5.78)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}=(\mathrm{d}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda,\mathrm{d}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}\,)\ .

For the second term, take a
T{\mathcal{B}}
-connection
\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}
on
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
for which
u=(\lambda,1)
is parallel:

(5.79)

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}u=\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}(\lambda,1)=(0,0)\ ,

and extend to the whole of
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
by compatibility with
\nabla
.

Let
\widehat{X}_{i}=(\dot{X}_{i},\ddot{X}_{i})
and
\widehat{\alpha}_{i}=(\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i})
, with
i=1,\ldots,\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{B}}
. Let
n=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}={\rm rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(L)
. Choose the basis
(g_{\mu})
for
{\mathscr{G}}=L\oplus L^{*}
such that
(g_{\mu})_{1\leqslant\mu\leqslant n}\subset L
and
(g_{\mu})_{n<\mu\leqslant 2n}\subset L^{*}
; then the dual basis
(\tilde{g}_{\mu})
is seen as a subset of
{\mathscr{G}}=L^{*}\oplus L
. Let
\widehat{g}_{\mu}=(\dot{g}_{\mu},\ddot{g}_{\mu})
and
\widehat{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}=(\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu},\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu})
. Let
\ell\in L^{*}
such that
\widehat{\ell}=(\dot{\ell},\ddot{\ell})\in\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}}
, and set
{\mathsf{s}}=\ell\cdot u
.
Then

(5.80)

\displaystyle\nabla\ell

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\alpha_{i}\wedge\nabla_{X_{i}}\ell

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\mathtt{p}_{\mathscr{G}}\big(\llbracket\dot{X}_{i},\dot{\ell}\,\rrbracket_{1},\llbracket\ddot{X}_{i},\ddot{\ell}\,\rrbracket_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\Big(\sum_{\mu\leqslant n}\,\big(\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\,(\iota_{[\dot{X}_{i},\dot{\ell}\,]}\dot{g}_{\mu}),\,\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\,(\iota_{\ddot{g}_{\mu}}\,\pounds_{\ddot{X}_{i}}\ddot{\ell}\,)\big)+\sum_{\mu>n}\,\big((\iota_{\dot{g}_{\mu}}\,\iota_{\dot{X}_{i}}\,\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\,\underline{H}{}_{1})\,\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu},\,0\big)\Big)\ .

Hence

(5.81)

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}{\mathsf{s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\alpha_{i}\wedge\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}_{X_{i}}{\mathsf{s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\alpha_{i}\wedge\nabla_{X_{i}}\ell\cdot u

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\Big(\sum_{\mu\leqslant n}\,\big(\iota_{[\dot{X}_{i},\dot{\ell}\,]}\dot{g}_{\mu}\,\iota_{\dot{g}_{\mu}}\lambda,\,\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\,(\iota_{\ddot{g}_{\mu}}\,\pounds_{\ddot{X}_{i}}\ddot{\ell}\,)\big)+\sum_{\mu>n}\,\big((\iota_{\dot{g}_{\mu}}\,\iota_{\dot{X}_{i}}\,\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\,\underline{H}{}_{1})\,\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\lambda\,,\,0\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\big(\iota_{[\dot{X}_{i},\dot{\ell}\,]}\lambda,\,\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\,(\iota_{\ddot{g}_{\mu}}\,\pounds_{\ddot{X}_{i}}\ddot{\ell}\,)\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\big(\iota_{\dot{X}_{i}}(\pounds_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda),\,\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\,(\iota_{\ddot{g}_{\mu}}\pounds_{\ddot{X}_{i}}\ddot{\ell}\,)\big)\ .

The term involving
\underline{H}{}_{1}
here vanishes since
\lambda\in\det{\mathscr{G}}_{1}^{\star}
and hence
\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\lambda=0
for all
\mu>n
. Since
\Omega^{\lambda}
is
D_{1}
-invariant, it follows that
\iota_{\dot{X}_{i}}(\pounds_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda)=0
and so
\nabla^{{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}}{\mathsf{s}}=0
for
{\mathsf{s}}=\ell\cdot u
, because
R
is a geometric T-duality relation. Thus the second term in (
5.76
) vanishes.

The third term is

(5.82)

\displaystyle-({\mathcal{H}}\wedge\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3!}\,\sum_{i,j,k}\,\big((\underline{H}{}_{1})_{ijk}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{k}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega,\,(\underline{H}{}_{2})_{ijk}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{k}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\big)\otimes\widehat{\mathsf{s}}

\displaystyle=-(\dot{h}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda,\,\ddot{h}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}\,)\ .

The fourth term is calculated as follows. Projecting with
\mathtt{p}_{2}
we get

(5.83)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathtt{p}_{2}\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})&=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathtt{p}_{2}\,\sum_{i,j,\mu}\,\langle\hbox{\hskip 411.22394pt\hskip-24.36862pt\hbox{$W(X_{i},X_{j})$}\hskip-24.36862pt\hskip-411.22394pt\raisebox{2.54445pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.11778pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{677201}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-206.03706pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03706pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03552pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-206.03593pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{44752315}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-411.22394pt\hskip 411.22394pt}\,,\,(\dot{g}_{\mu},\ddot{g}_{\mu})\rangle_{R_{\mathcal{B}}}\ (\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i})\wedge(\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j},\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j})\wedge(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega)\\[-10.0pt]
&\hskip 227.62204pt\otimes(\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu},\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu})\cdot(\dot{\ell},\ddot{\ell}\,)\cdot(\lambda,1)\\[4.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j,\mu}\,\langle\llbracket\ddot{X}_{i},\ddot{X}_{j}\rrbracket_{2}\,,\,\ddot{g}_{\mu}\rangle_{2}\ \pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\cdot\ddot{\ell}\\[4.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i,j}\,(\underline{H}{}_{2})_{ij\mu}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\ddot{\ell}\\
&\qquad\,+\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu>n,i,j}\,\ddot{g}_{\mu}([\ddot{X}_{i},\ddot{X}_{j}])\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}}\ddot{\ell}\\[4.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i,j}\,(\underline{H}{}_{2})_{ij\mu}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\ddot{\ell}\\
&\qquad\,+\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}([\ddot{X}_{i},\ddot{X}_{j}])\\[4.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i,j}\,(\underline{H}{}_{2})_{ij\mu}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\ddot{\ell}\\
&\qquad\,-\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}\ddot{\ell}(\ddot{X}_{i},\ddot{X}_{j})\ .\end{split}

Similarly, projecting with
\mathtt{p}_{1}
yields

(5.84)

\displaystyle\mathtt{p}_{1}\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j,\mu}\,\langle\llbracket\dot{X}_{i},\dot{X}_{j}\rrbracket_{1}\,,\,\dot{g}_{\mu}\rangle_{1}\ \pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\cdot\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda

(5.85)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu>n,i,j}\,(\underline{H}{}_{1})_{ij\mu}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda

(5.86)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu\leqslant n,i,j}\,\dot{g}_{\mu}([\dot{X}_{i},\dot{X}_{j}])\,\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\cdot\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda

(5.87)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\mu>n,i,j}\,(\underline{H}{}_{1})_{ij\mu}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\dot{\tilde{g}}_{\mu}\wedge\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda

(5.88)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{i,j}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{i}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\alpha_{j}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}(\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda)(\dot{X}_{i},\dot{X}_{j})\ .

It follows that the third, fourth and fifth terms combine to give

(5.89)

\displaystyle-({\mathcal{H}}\wedge\omega)\otimes{\mathsf{s}}+(-1)^{|\omega|+1}\,\overline{W}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})+\tfrac{1}{4}\,(-1)^{|\omega|+1}\,\omega\otimes C\cdot{\mathsf{s}}

\displaystyle=\big(-\underline{H}{}_{1}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda+(-1)^{|\omega|}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}\,\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda\,,\,-\underline{H}{}_{2}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}+(-1)^{|\omega|}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}\ddot{\ell}\,\big)\ .

Hence

(5.90)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{D}}(\omega\otimes{\mathsf{s}})

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{d}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda+(-1)^{|\omega|}\,\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}\,\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda-\underline{H}{}_{1}\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes\iota_{\dot{\ell}}\lambda\ ,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}+(-1)^{|\omega|}\,\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\mathrm{d}\ddot{\ell}-\underline{H}{}_{2}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes\ddot{\ell}\,\big)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}}\times\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})(\widehat{\omega}\otimes\widehat{\mathsf{s}}\,)\ .

Since
\mathfrak{k}_{\tau_{1}}
spans
\mathtt{p}_{1}(L^{*})
pointwise, this can be extended to the whole of
L^{*}
, which by the Leibniz rule can then be extended to the whole of
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
and hence
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
.
∎

Example 5.91

(
Torus Bundles
)
.

Continuing from Example
5.65
, the fact that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
-DGO relation is equivalent to the fact that
\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}}\varrho(\omega)=(-1)^{k}\,\varrho(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}}\omega)
for all
\omega\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
. The existence of
\lambda
satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
5.77
is guaranteed here: if
\theta_{1}
is a connection on the principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-bundle
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}
and
(t_{a})
is a basis of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}^{k}
of
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
, then
\theta_{1}=\sum_{a}\,\theta_{1}^{a}\otimes t_{a}
where
\theta_{1}^{a}\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{1}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
for
a=1,\dots,k
. Then
\lambda=\theta_{1}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{1}^{k}
is
D_{1}
-invariant.

Example 5.92

(
Poisson-Lie T-Duality
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie algebra with a non-degenerate pairing,
{\mathsf{G}}
a connected Lie group integrating
\mathfrak{g}
, and let
{\mathsf{H}}_{1},{\mathsf{H}}_{2}\subset{\mathsf{G}}
be subgroups whose Lie subalgebras
\mathfrak{h}_{1},\mathfrak{h}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{g}
are Lagrangian. There is then the T-duality relation
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
between exact Courant algebroids
E_{1}
over
{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{H}}_{1}
and
E_{2}
over
{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{H}}_{2}

[
55
,
22
]
. If
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
is a generalised isometry, then the existence of an invariant volume form
\lambda
on
{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{H}}_{1}
is equivalent to
\mathfrak{h}_{1}
being a unimodular Lie algebra. We may thus construct the
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
. The unimodularity condition coincides with the existence of an invariant divergence on
{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{H}}_{1}

[
47
, Example 2.5]
.

5.4.
T-Duality Relations and Type II Supergravity

The bosonic field content of Type II supergravity on a manifold
M
is given by a quadruple
(g,H,\phi,F)
of a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
, a closed three-form
H
(the Kalb-Ramond flux), a smooth function
\phi\in C^{\infty}(M)
(the dilaton), and an inhomogenous differential form
F\in{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}(M)
of even or odd degree which is closed under the twisted differential
\mathrm{d}_{H}
(the collection of Ramond-Ramond fluxes). The form
F
is also required to satisfy an additional self-duality condition, which we discuss below.

The equations of motion for Type II supergravity can then be formulated on the twisted standard Courant algebroid
(\mathbb{T}M,H)
equipped with the generalised metric
V_{g}^{+}
corresponding to
g
, the divergence
{\rm div}_{\phi}
corresponding to
\phi
, and the spinor bundle
{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}(M)
. They are given by

(5.93)

\displaystyle\mathrm{GRic}_{g,{\rm div}_{\phi}}(u_{+},v_{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{8\,\nu}\,(\!(u_{+}\cdot F,v_{-}\cdot F)\!)\ ,\quad\forall\,u_{+}\in V_{g}^{+}\ ,\ v_{-}\in V_{g}^{-}\ ,

(5.94)

\displaystyle\mathrm{GR}_{g,{\rm div}_{\phi}}

\displaystyle=0\ .

Here
(\!(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)\!)
is the pairing on spinors discussed in Subsection
5.4.1
below,
\mathrm{GRic}_{g,{\rm div}_{\phi}}
is the generalised Ricci tensor associated to the pair
(V^{+}_{g},{\rm div}_{\phi})
as in
[
47
, Definition 3.1]
, and
\mathrm{GR}_{g,{\rm div}_{\phi}}
is the generalised scalar curvature as in
[
52
, Definition 4.9]
.
Equations (
5.93
) and (
5.94
) are the equations of motion of the Type IIA theory if
\nu=-1
and
F
is of even degree, whereas when
\nu=\mathrm{i}
and
F
is of odd degree they are the equations of motion of the Type IIB theory.

5.4.1.
Pairing on Spinors

Recalling Remark
2.17
, we define a pairing on the space

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}={\mathsf{S}}\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2}}

by

(5.95)

\displaystyle(\!(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)\!)

\displaystyle\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{C}\ ,\qquad(\!({\mathsf{s}}\otimes\mu,{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}\otimes\mu)\!)=\langle(\vartheta\,{\mathsf{s}}\wedge{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime})^{\rm top},\mu^{2}\rangle\ ,

where here
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle
denotes the contraction between
\det{\mathsf{S}}
and
\det{\mathsf{S}}^{*}
, and
\vartheta
is the operator defined by

\vartheta\,{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{i}^{\,|{\mathsf{s}}|}\,{\mathsf{s}}\ .

In the setting of Subsection
5.3.1
with the splitting (
5.49
) of
R
, recall that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}
, where
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mathscr{G}}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}L^{*}\cdot(\lambda,1)
. Let

(5.96)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{R}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\otimes|\det T{\mathcal{B}}\,|^{\frac{1}{2}}\otimes\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}L^{*}\cdot(\lambda,1)\otimes\left|\big(\det L^{*}\cdot(\lambda,1)\big)^{*}\cdot(\lambda^{\prime},1)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\ ,

regarded as a subbundle

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{R}\ \subset\ \big[\big((\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\otimes|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\big((\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})\otimes|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big]\big|_{\underline{C}}\ ,

where
\lambda^{\prime}\in\det T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
satisfies
\iota_{\lambda^{\prime}}\,\lambda=1
.
Since
L^{*}
is chosen so that
\underline{\rho}{}_{2}(\mathtt{p}_{2}(L^{*}))=\set{0}
, it follows that
(\det L^{*}\cdot(\lambda,1))^{*}
is generated by
(1,{}^{R}\lambda)
for
{}^{R}\lambda\in\det T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}
satisfying
(\lambda,{}^{R}\lambda)\in\mathsf{Cl}(R)
. We then write

\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}\approx_{R}\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}\qquad\text{if}\quad(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{R}\ .

Proposition 5.97
.

Let
R\colon\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
be a T-duality relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
given by Equation (
5.62
) and
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{R}
as in Equation (
5.96
).
If
\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}\approx_{R}\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\,\prime}\approx_{R}\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\,\prime}
, we write

\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{i}={\mathsf{s}}_{i}\otimes\mu_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{i}^{\,\prime}={\mathsf{s}}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes\mu_{i}

for
i=1,2
, where
({\mathsf{s}}_{1},{\mathsf{s}}_{2}),({\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime},{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\prime})\in{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
and
\mu_{i}\in|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Then

(5.98)

\displaystyle(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\,\prime})\!)_{1}=\mathrm{i}^{\,n}\,(-1)^{n^{2}+n\,|{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime}|}\,(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\,\prime})\!)_{2}

in
\mathbbm{C}
, where
n=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
and
(\!(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)\!)_{i}
for
i=1,2
are the pairings on spinors on each related Courant algebroid.

Proof.

Let
c\in\underline{C}
. We can write

{\mathsf{s}}_{1}=\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes(\ell\cdot\lambda)\quad,\quad{\mathsf{s}}_{2}=\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes{}^{R}\ell\quad,\quad{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime}=\pi_{1}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes(\ell^{\prime}\cdot\lambda)\quad,\quad{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\prime}=\pi_{2}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes{}^{R}\ell^{\prime}\ ,

where
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
and
\ell,\ell^{\prime},{}^{R}\ell,{}^{R}\ell^{\prime}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}L^{*}
. We can also write
\mu_{2}\in|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
as

\mu_{2}=\ddot{\mu}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes\sqrt{{}^{R}\lambda}\ ,

for
\mu_{\mathcal{B}}\in|\det T{\mathcal{B}}\,|^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\sqrt{{}^{R}\lambda}\in\mathtt{p}_{2}\big(|\det L^{*}\cdot(\lambda,1)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)
such that
\big(\sqrt{{}^{R}\lambda}\,\big)^{2}={}^{R}\lambda
. Similarly, we write
\mu_{1}\in|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
as

\mu_{1}=\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\sqrt{\lambda^{\prime}}\ .

Assume that
|\omega|+|\omega^{\prime}|=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{B}}
and
|\ell|+|\ell^{\prime}|=|{}^{R}\ell|+|{}^{R}\ell^{\prime}|=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}=:n
, so that the pairings are non-zero.

Then

(5.99)

\displaystyle(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\,\prime})\!)_{2}

\displaystyle=(\!({\mathsf{s}}_{2}\otimes\ddot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\sqrt{{}^{R}\lambda}\,,\,{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\prime}\otimes\ddot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\sqrt{{}^{R}\lambda}\,)\!)_{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+|\ell|}\,(\pi_{2}^{*}\omega\otimes{}^{R}\ell)\wedge(\pi_{2}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes{}^{R}\ell^{\prime})\,,\,\ddot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\otimes{}^{R}\lambda\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+|\ell|}\,(-1)^{|\ell|\,|\omega^{\prime}|}\,\langle\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime},\mu_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\rangle\,\langle{}^{R}\ell\wedge{}^{R}\ell^{\prime},{}^{R}\lambda\rangle\ .

Similarly

(5.100)

\displaystyle(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\,\prime})\!)_{1}

\displaystyle=(\!({\mathsf{s}}_{1}\otimes\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\sqrt{\lambda^{\prime}}\,,\,{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime}\otimes\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\sqrt{\lambda^{\prime}}\,)\!)_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+n-|\ell|}\,\langle(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\otimes(\ell\cdot\lambda))\wedge(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\otimes(\ell^{\prime}\cdot\lambda))\,,\,\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\otimes\lambda^{\prime}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+n-|\ell|}\,(-1)^{(n-|\ell|)\,|\omega^{\prime}|}\,\langle(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega\wedge\pi_{1}^{*}\omega^{\prime}\,)\otimes((\ell\cdot\lambda)\wedge(\ell^{\prime}\cdot\lambda))\,,\,\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\otimes\lambda^{\prime}\,\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+n-|\ell|}\,(-1)^{(n-|\ell|)\,|\omega^{\prime}|}\,(-1)^{|\ell|\,(n-|\ell|)}\,\langle\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime},\mu_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\rangle\,\langle(\ell\cdot\ell^{\prime}\cdot\lambda)\,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\,\rangle\ .

By construction

\ell\cdot\ell^{\prime}\cdot\lambda\ \sim_{R}\ (-1)^{\frac{n\,(n+1)}{2}}\,\iota_{{}^{R}\lambda}\big({}^{R}\ell\wedge{}^{R}\ell^{\prime}\,\big)

in
\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}}_{c})
. Since both sides of this
R
-relation are real numbers, and
(r_{1},r_{2})\in\mathsf{Cl}({\mathscr{G}}_{c})
for real numbers
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbbm{R}
if and only if
r_{1}=r_{2}
, it follows that

(5.101)

\displaystyle(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\,\prime})\!)_{1}=a\,(\!(\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2},\widetilde{\mathsf{s}}_{2}^{\,\prime})\!)_{2}

where

(5.102)

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,|\omega|+n-|\ell|-|\omega|-|\ell|}\,(-1)^{n\,|\omega^{\prime}|-|\ell|\,|\omega^{\prime}|+n\,|\ell|-|\ell|^{2}-|\ell|\,|\omega^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{\,n}\,(-1)^{|\ell|+n\,(|{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime}|+|\ell|-n)+n\,|\ell|-|\ell|^{2}}=\mathrm{i}^{\,n}\,(-1)^{n^{2}+n\,|{\mathsf{s}}_{1}^{\prime}|}\ ,

and the result follows.
∎

5.4.2.
Self-Dual Spinors and T-Duality

Let
R:(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},V_{1}^{+})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},V_{2}^{+})
be a geometric T-duality relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
of parity
j=n\mod 2
, where
n=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}
. Let
d=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}=\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
.
Choose a basis
\set{r_{1},\ldots,r_{d}}
for
R^{+}
such that
\set{e_{1},\dots,e_{d}}:=\set{\mathtt{p}_{1}(r_{1}),\dots,\mathtt{p}_{1}(r_{d})}
is an orthonormal basis for
V_{1}^{+}
. Then
\set{f_{1},\dots,f_{d}}:=\set{\mathtt{p}_{2}(r_{1}),\dots,\mathtt{p}_{2}(r_{d})}
is an orthonormal basis for
V_{2}^{+}
. Define

{\mathcal{R}}_{V_{1}^{+}}=2^{\frac{d}{2}}\ e_{1}\cdot\,\cdots\,\cdot e_{d}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{R}}_{V_{2}^{+}}=2^{\frac{d}{2}}\ f_{1}\cdot\,\cdots\,\cdot f_{d}\ .

Then

(5.103)

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{R}}_{V_{1}^{+}}\cdot u_{1}\,,\,(-1)^{n\,d}\ {\mathcal{R}}_{V_{2}^{+}}\cdot u_{2}\big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{R}\ ,

for all
(u_{1},u_{2})\in{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
.

Thus if
u_{1}
satisfies the self-duality equation

{\mathcal{R}}_{V_{1}^{+}}\cdot u_{1}=u_{1}\ ,

then

(5.104)

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{R}}_{V_{1}^{+}}\cdot u_{1}\,,\,(-1)^{n\,d}\ {\mathcal{R}}_{V_{2}^{+}}\cdot u_{2}\big)=\big(u_{1}\,,\,(-1)^{n\,d}\ {\mathcal{R}}_{V_{2}^{+}}\cdot u_{2}\big)\ \in{\mathsf{S}}_{R}

implies the (anti-)self-duality equation

(5.105)

\displaystyle(-1)^{n\,d}\ {\mathcal{R}}_{V_{2}^{+}}\cdot u_{2}=u_{2}\ ,

by Corollary
5.5
.
In Type II supergravity,
d=10
and hence
u_{2}
also satisfies the self-duality equation.

In the context of generalised geometry, we replace the space of spinors
{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})
with the space

\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Omega}}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})={\mathsf{\Omega}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\otimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(|\det T{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)

in Equations (
5.93
) and (
5.94
) with labels
i=A,B
, giving the
modified Type II supergravity equations
, see
[
47
, Section 7.1]
.

Proposition 5.106
.

Let
(g_{A},\underline{H}{}_{A},\phi_{A},F_{A})
and
(g_{B},\underline{H}{}_{B},\phi_{B},F_{B})
be Type II bosonic fields on T-dual principal circle bundles
{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}\to{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{B}\to{\mathcal{B}}
over the same base
{\mathcal{B}}
, respectively. Let

(5.107)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{A},\underline{H}{}_{A},V^{+}_{g_{A}},{\rm div}_{\phi_{A}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{B},\underline{H}{}_{B},V^{+}_{g_{B}},{\rm div}_{\phi_{B}})}

be a geometric T-duality relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
such that

F_{A}\approx_{R}F_{B}\ .

Then
(g_{A},\underline{H}{}_{A},\phi_{A},F_{A})
satisfies the equations of motion (
5.93
)–(
5.94
) for
\nu=-1
if and only if
(g_{B},\underline{H}{}_{B},\phi_{B},F_{B})
satisfies the equations of motion (
5.93
)–(
5.94
) for
\nu=\mathrm{i}
.

Proof.

In the Type IIA theory,
F_{A}
is of even degree and hence
|u_{A}{}_{\pm}\cdot F_{A}|=1\mod 2
for all
u_{A}{}_{\pm}\in V_{g_{A}}^{\pm}
. Thus by Proposition
5.97
with
n=1
we get

(5.108)

\displaystyle(\!(u_{A}{}_{+}\cdot F_{A},v_{A}{}_{-}\cdot F_{A})\!)=\mathrm{i}\ (\!(u_{B}{}_{+}\cdot F_{B},v_{B}{}_{-}\cdot F_{B})\!)\ .

Since
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is degree shifting by
\pm 1
,
F_{B}
is of odd degree.

We know from
[
22
, Theorem 4.14]
that the generalised Ricci tensors and generalised scalar curvatures are preserved by the T-duality relation
R
.
Thus if
(g_{A},\underline{H}{}_{A},\phi_{A},F_{A})
satisfies the Type IIA supergravity equations, then

(5.109)

\displaystyle\mathrm{GRic}_{g_{B},{\rm div}_{\phi_{B}}}(u_{B}{}_{+},v_{B}{}_{-})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{GRic}_{g_{A},{\rm div}_{\phi_{A}}}(u_{A}{}_{+},v_{A}{}_{-})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{8}\,(\!(u_{A}{}_{+}\cdot F_{A},v_{A}{}_{-}\cdot F_{A})\!)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\,(\!(u_{B}{}_{+}\cdot F_{B},v_{B}{}_{-}\cdot F_{B})\!)\ ,

and
\mathrm{GR}_{g_{B},{\rm div}_{\phi_{B}}}=\mathrm{GR}_{g_{A},{\rm div}_{\phi_{A}}}=0
, hence
(g_{B},\underline{H}{}_{B},\phi_{B},F_{B})
is a solution of the Type IIB supergravity equations.

Conversely, if the Type IIB equations of motion are satisfied by
(g_{B},\underline{H}{}_{B},\phi_{B},F_{B})
, then since
|u_{B}{}_{\pm}\cdot F_{B}|=0\mod 2
, we find

(5.110)

\displaystyle\mathrm{GRic}_{g_{A},{\rm div}_{\phi_{A}}}(u_{A}{}_{+},v_{A}{}_{-})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{GRic}_{g_{B},{\rm div}_{\phi_{B}}}(u_{B}{}_{+},v_{B}{}_{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\,(\!(u_{B}{}_{+}\cdot F_{B},v_{B}{}_{-}\cdot F_{B})\!)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\,\mathrm{i}}\,(\!(u_{A}{}_{+}\cdot F_{A},v_{A}{}_{-}\cdot F_{A})\!)\ ,

as required.

We have already seen that the self-duality equation is preserved.
Since
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{A}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{B}})
-DGO relation, it follows that
\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{A}}F_{A}=0
if and only if
\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{B}}F_{B}=0
.
∎

6.
Generalised Complex Relations

In this section we consider how generalised complex structures behave under Courant algebroid relations. We will see that a generalised complex relation has an induced grading, and we will write an explicit formula for the type change of generalised complex structures under geometric T-duality.

6.1.
Relating Generalised Complex Structures

Suppose that
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are Courant algebroids respectively endowed with generalised almost complex structures
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}.

The product Courant algebroid
E_{1}\times E_{2}
is endowed with the generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{J}}_{2}.

As before, when working with generalised complex structures one introduces the complexified Courant algebroids in order to discuss the
\pm\,\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundles, but we shall often suppress the complexification in the notation. All previous results concerning Dirac relations and spinor relations hold in the complex case too.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation supported on
C\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2}
. Let
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
be generalised almost complex structures on
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
respectively.
Then
R
is a
generalised almost complex relation
if

({\mathcal{J}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{J}}_{2})(R)=R\ .

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
are generalised complex structures, then
R
is a
generalised complex relation
.

A first instance of this definition appeared in
[
6
]
with
C
being the graph of a smooth map.

Example 6.2
.

Let
R=\text{gr}(\Phi)\colon E_{1}\rightarrowtail E_{2}
be a classical Courant algebroid morphism supported on
\text{gr}(\varphi)\subseteq M_{1}\times M_{2},
for some vector bundle morphism
\Phi\colon E_{1}\to E_{2}
covering a smooth map
\varphi\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}.
Suppose that
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are endowed with the generalised complex structures
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2},
respectively. An element
(e_{1},e_{2})\in E_{1}\times E_{2}
is also in
R
if and only if

(e_{1},e_{2})=(e_{1},\Phi(e_{1}))\in R
. Thus by Definition
6.1

(6.3)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{J}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{J}}_{2})\big(e_{1},\Phi(e_{1})\big)=\big({\mathcal{J}}_{1}(e_{1}),{\mathcal{J}}_{2}(\Phi(e_{1}))\big)

is again in
R
if and only if

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2}\circ\Phi=\Phi\circ{\mathcal{J}}_{1}\ ,

as expected for a well-defined notion of morphism of generalised complex structures. This condition is equivalent to

(6.5)

\displaystyle\Phi(L_{1})\ \subseteq\ L_{2}\ ,

where
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are the
+\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundles of
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2},
respectively. If
\Phi
is an isomorphism, this last condition becomes
\Phi(L_{1})=L_{2}.

Example 6.6
.

In the setting of Example
6.2
, let
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
be given by a symplectic structure
\omega
on
M_{1}
(cf. Example
3.71
). Then

L_{1}=\set{X-\mathrm{i}\,\iota_{X}\omega\ |\ X\in TM_{1}}\ .

With
\Phi={\,\rm e}\,^{B}\circ\big(\varphi_{*}\oplus(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\big)
, it follows that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\Phi(L_{1})

\displaystyle=\set{\varphi_{*}X+B(\varphi_{*}X)-(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{i}\,\iota_{X}\omega)\ |\ X\in TM_{1}}

\displaystyle=\set{Y+B(Y)-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\iota_{Y}\,(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\omega)\ |\ Y\in TM_{2}}\ .

Thus
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is a
B
-twist of the symplectic structure
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\omega
on
M_{2}
.

Remark 6.8
.

If
R\colon(E_{1},{\mathcal{J}}_{1})\dashrightarrow(E_{2},{\mathcal{J}}_{2})
is a generalised complex relation and
(k,0)\in{\rm K}_{R}
, then
({\mathcal{J}}_{1}(k),0)\in{\rm K}_{R}
and hence
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
preserves
\ker(R)
. Similarly
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
preserves
{\rm coker}(R)
.

Definition 6.9
.

A Courant algebroid relation
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
is a
generalised almost complex relation
if at each point
c\in C
there is a splitting

(6.10)

\displaystyle R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}=R^{+\mathrm{i}}_{c}\oplus R^{-\mathrm{i}}_{c}\ ,

where

R^{+\mathrm{i}}_{c}=R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})_{c}\qquad\text{and}\qquad R^{-\mathrm{i}}_{c}=R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}\cap(\overline{L}_{1}\times\overline{L}_{2})_{c}\ .

Again we drop the adjective
“almost”
when
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
are generalised complex structures.

Remark 6.11
.

Since
R
is a real vector bundle (so that
\overline{R^{\mathbbm{C}}}=R^{\mathbbm{C}}
), it follows that
\overline{R^{\pm\mathrm{i}}}\subset R^{\mp\mathrm{i}}
and hence
R^{\mp\mathrm{i}}\subset\overline{R^{\pm\mathrm{i}}}
. Thus
\overline{R^{\pm\mathrm{i}}}=R^{\mp\mathrm{i}}
and hence

\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{C}}(R^{\pm\mathrm{i}})=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{C}}(R^{\mathbbm{C}})\ .

In particular, the rank of a generalised almost complex relation
R
is necessarily even.

Similarly to
[
21
, Proposition 4.15]
, which establishes equivalence of two definitions of generalised isometries (cf. Definition
4.11
), we can show

Proposition 6.12
.

Definitions
6.1
and
6.9
are equivalent.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{J}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
. If
{\mathcal{J}}(R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c})=R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}
and
r\in R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}
, then
r^{\pm\mathrm{i}}:=\frac{1}{2}\,(r\mp\mathrm{i}\,{\mathcal{J}}(r))\in R_{c}^{\pm\mathrm{i}}
with
r=r^{+\mathrm{i}}+r^{-\mathrm{i}}
, and hence
R_{c}^{\mathbbm{C}}\subset R^{+\mathrm{i}}_{c}\oplus R^{-\mathrm{i}}_{c}
. The converse easily follows.
∎

From Proposition
5.38
we immediately get

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
R\colon(E_{1},{\mathcal{J}}_{1})\dashrightarrow(E_{2},{\mathcal{J}}_{2})
be a generalised almost complex relation, with
\mathtt{p}_{2}(R)\supset L_{2}
(resp.
\mathtt{p}_{1}(R)\supset L_{1}
). If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
(resp.
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
) is a generalised complex structure (i.e.
L_{1}
(resp.
L_{2}
) is integrable), then
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
(resp.
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
) is a generalised complex structure, and hence
R
is a generalised complex relation.

Remark 6.14
.

If
R\colon(E_{1},L_{1})\dashrightarrow(E_{2},L_{2})
is a generalised almost complex relation, then one automatically has
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
and
\overline{L}_{1}\sim_{R}\overline{L}_{2}
. The converse is also true, because
\overline{R^{\mathbbm{C}}\rvert_{L_{1}}}=R^{\mathbbm{C}}\rvert_{\overline{L}_{1}}
and hence

(6.15)

\displaystyle R^{\mathbbm{C}}\big\rvert_{\overline{L}_{1}}=R^{\mathbbm{C}}\cap(\overline{L}_{1}\times\overline{L}_{2})+{\rm CK}_{R}

since
R
is real, and noting that
{\rm CK}_{R}
is also real. Thus there is a third equivalent definition:
R
is a generalised almost complex relation if and only if
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
for the complex almost Dirac structures generated by
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
. Therefore, by Proposition
5.22
we obtain

Proposition 6.16
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation, and let
{\mathcal{J}}_{i}
be generalised almost complex structures on
E_{i}
with associated pure spinor line bundles
\Omega_{i}
for
i=1,2
. If
\Omega_{1}\sim_{R}\Omega_{2}
, then
R
is a generalised almost complex relation.

Let
(E_{1},L_{1})
and
(E_{2},L_{2})
be Courant algebroids equipped with complex Lagrangian structures, and let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a Courant algebroid relation. Recalling the kernel and cokernel subbundles
{\rm K}_{R}
and
{\rm CK}_{R}
of
R
from Definition
4.6
, we define the subbundle

(6.17)

\displaystyle R^{+\mathrm{i}}_{c}{}^{\times}=\big[\big(R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}\cap(L_{1}\times L_{2})_{c}+{\rm K}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}{}_{c}+{\rm CK}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}{}_{c}\big)\,\big/\,{\rm K}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}{}_{c}\big]\,\big/\,{\rm CK}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}{}_{c}

and similarly for
R^{-\mathrm{i}}_{c}{}^{\times}
.

Definition 6.18
.

A Courant algebroid relation
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
is a
weak generalised almost complex relation
if at each point
c\in C
there is a splitting

(6.19)

\displaystyle R^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}{}^{\times}=R^{+\mathrm{i}}_{c}{}^{\times}\oplus R^{-\mathrm{i}}_{c}{}^{\times}\ .

Similarly to Proposition
2.29
, it follows that if
R
is a generalised almost complex relation then it is necessarily a weak generalised almost complex relation.

The grading induced by a generalised complex structure from (
3.75
) similarly induces a grading of
R
-Clifford relations according to

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
R\colon(E_{1},{\mathcal{J}}_{1})\dashrightarrow(E_{2},{\mathcal{J}}_{2})
be a generalised almost complex relation with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
. There is a grading

(6.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{\mathbbm{C}}={\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n}\oplus{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n-1}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{R}

where

{\mathcal{S}}^{k}_{R}=\big((\Omega^{k}_{1}\times\Omega_{2}^{k})\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}\big)\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}\ ,

with
\Omega_{i}^{k}
as in (
3.75
) for
k\in\set{0,\ldots,n}
and
i=1,2
.

Proof.

Since
L_{1}\sim_{R}L_{2}
, it follows that
\overline{L}_{1}\sim_{R}\overline{L}_{2}
. Define
{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n}=\big((\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2})\otimes|\det{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}\big)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{\mathbbm{C}}
and

(6.22)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n-k}=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k}R^{-\mathrm{i}}\cdot{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n}\ ,

for
k=1,\dots,n
.
Since
{\mathcal{S}}_{R}
is invariant under the Clifford action of
R
, it follows that
{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n-k}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{\mathbbm{C}}
. The projections
\mathtt{p}_{i}({\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{n-k})=\Omega_{i}^{n-k}
ensure that the sum (
6.21
) is direct, and since
R
is a Dirac relation between
\overline{L}_{1}
and
\overline{L}_{2}
this ensures equality.
∎

Let

\mathrm{d}_{H_{i}}=\partial_{H_{i}}+\overline{\partial}_{H_{i}}

be the decompositions of the Dirac generating operators associated with generalised complex structures
{\mathcal{J}}_{i}
.

Proposition 6.23
.

Let
R\colon E_{1}\dashrightarrow E_{2}
be a generalised complex relation, with
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
of parity
j
such that
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is a
(\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}})
-DGO relation. Then

(6.24)

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{H_{1}}\times(-1)^{j}\,\partial_{H_{2}}\big)({\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R})\ \subset\ {\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big(\overline{\partial}_{H_{1}}\times(-1)^{j}\,\overline{\partial}_{H_{2}}\big)({\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R})\ \subset\ {\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}\ .

Proof.

Let
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{R}^{k}
for
k\in\set{0,\dots,n}
, where we suppress the determinant part from the notation. Then
r=(\mathrm{d}_{H_{1}}\alpha_{1},(-1)^{j}\,\mathrm{d}_{H_{2}}\alpha_{2})\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R}
, and so there is a decomposition
r=r_{0}+\cdots+r_{n}
from (
6.21
). Since
\mathrm{d}_{H_{i}}({\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\Omega_{i}^{k}))\subset{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\Omega_{i}^{k-1})\oplus{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\Omega_{i}^{k+1})
, it follows that
r=r_{k-1}+r_{k+1}
and hence

(6.25)

\displaystyle\partial_{H_{1}}\times(-1)^{j}\,\partial_{H_{2}}\colon{\mathcal{S}}^{k}_{R}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}^{k-1}_{R}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{\partial}_{H_{1}}\times(-1)^{j}\,\overline{\partial}_{H_{2}}\colon{\mathcal{S}}^{k}_{R}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}^{k+1}_{R}\ ,

thus the result follows.
∎

6.2.
Generalised Complex Reduction

Consider the reduction of a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
to a generalised almost complex structure
\underline{{\mathcal{J}}}
from Subsection
3.3.1
.
Then by Remark
6.8
, the reduction morphism
Q(K)
of Example
4.8
is a generalised almost complex relation from
{\mathcal{J}}
to
\underline{{\mathcal{J}}}
if and only if
{\mathcal{J}}(K)=K
. This can be formulated in terms of the Lagrangian subbundle
L\subset E^{\mathbbm{C}}
corresponding to
{\mathcal{J}}
, where

(6.26)

\displaystyle Q(K)^{\mathbbm{C}}=Q(K)^{+\mathrm{i}}\oplus Q(K)^{-\mathrm{i}}\ \iff\ (K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}=\big(L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\oplus\big(\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\ .

The final condition is equivalent to
{\mathcal{J}}(K^{\perp})=K^{\perp}
, and hence
{\mathcal{J}}(K)=K
.

We shall explore relations coming from generalised complex reduction in their full generality, i.e. when the generalised complex reduction may satisfy
{\mathcal{J}}(K)\neq K
.

Proposition 6.27
.

Consider the setting of Proposition
3.79
for the generalised almost complex reduction of
(E,{\mathcal{J}})
. Then the reduction morphism
Q(K)
is a weak generalised almost complex relation.

Proof.

Set

L_{K}=\big(L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\,\big/\,K^{\mathbbm{C}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{L}_{K}=\big(\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\,\big/\,K^{\mathbbm{C}}\ .

Then

(6.28)

\displaystyle Q(K)_{(m,\varpi(m))}^{+\mathrm{i}\,\times}=\set{\big(\ell,\mathscr{J}^{-1}_{\varpi(m),m}(\ell)\big)\ |\ \ell\in L_{K}{}_{m}}

and similarly for the complex conjugate. Thus
Q(K)
is a weak generalised almost complex relation if and only if

(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}\,\big/\,K^{\mathbbm{C}}=L_{K}\oplus\overline{L}_{K}\ .

Since
L
is Lagrangian in
E^{\mathbbm{C}}
it follows that
L_{K}
is Lagrangian in
(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}/K^{\mathbbm{C}}
. Similarly
\overline{L}_{K}
is Lagrangian in
(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}/K^{\mathbbm{C}}
. The condition that their intersection is zero is given by

(6.29)

\displaystyle\big(L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\cap\big(\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\ \subset\ K^{\mathbbm{C}}\ .

In
[
10
, Lemma 5.1]
it is shown that Equation (
6.29
) is equivalent to the condition
{\mathcal{J}}(K)\cap K^{\perp}\subset K
, which is one of the requirements of Proposition
3.79
. This condition appears in
[
57
, Proposition 6.1]
, generalising
[
10
, Theorem 5.2]
.
∎

Consider a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}
on
E
. Recall from Section
3.3
that the associated pure spinor is given by

\psi=\exp(\alpha)\wedge\Theta\ ,

for a complex two-form
\alpha
and a decomposable complex
k
-form
\Theta
, where
k={\rm type}({\mathcal{J}})
. Let us examine how the reduction of spinors proceeds in the examples of symplectic (Example
3.71
) and complex (Example
3.65
) generalised complex structures.

Example 6.30
.

Suppose

\psi=\exp(\alpha)

and
\alpha
satisfies
\iota_{Y}\,\iota_{Z}\alpha=0
for all
Y,Z\in K^{\mathbbm{C}}=T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
, but there is some
X\in T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
such that
\iota_{X}\alpha\neq 0
and
\iota_{X}\alpha
is real. Then

(6.31)

\displaystyle(X-\iota_{X}\alpha)\cdot\exp(\alpha)=0

so that
X-\iota_{X}\alpha\in L
. Since
\iota_{Y}\,\iota_{X}\alpha=0
for each
Y\in T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
, it follows that
X-\iota_{X}\alpha\in L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}
. Thus
\iota_{X}\alpha\in L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}
and
\overline{\iota_{X}\alpha}\in\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}
. Since
\iota_{X}\alpha
is real, this implies

\iota_{X}\alpha\ \in\ \big(L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\cap\big(\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\ ,

but
\iota_{X}\alpha
does not live in
K^{\mathbbm{C}}
. Hence such a two-form
\alpha
cannot reduce to a generalised almost complex structure on
\underline{E}
.

Assume now that the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
has compact orientable fibres, and suppose that

\psi=\exp(\mathrm{i}\,\omega)

for a real two-form
\omega.

By the above arguments, there is no
X\in T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
such that
\iota_{X}\omega\in\mathrm{Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}})
and
\iota_{X}\omega
is non-zero.
It follows that
\omega
decomposes as

\omega=\omega_{\mathcal{Q}}+\omega_{\mathcal{F}}\ ,

where
\omega_{\mathcal{Q}}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}\mathrm{Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}})
and
\iota_{X}\,\iota_{Y}\omega_{\mathcal{F}}\neq 0
for every pointwise linearly independent
X,Y\in T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
.
Take

\mu=\frac{\omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{k}}{k!}\ \in\ \det(T^{*}{\mathcal{F}})\ ,

where
k=\frac{1}{2}\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{F}}
, and
{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}
as in Example
5.12
.

If
\psi
is
T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
-invariant in the sense of Definition
5.26
, then
\omega_{\mathcal{Q}}
is a basic two-form and
\int_{\mathcal{F}}\,\exp(\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\mathcal{F}})
is a basic function. Thus
\Omega^{\psi}\subset A^{\mathbbm{C}}_{\mu}
, and

\Big(\exp(\mathrm{i}\,\omega)\,,\,\big(\text{\footnotesize{$\int_{\mathcal{F}}$}}\,\exp(\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{{\mathcal{F}}})\big)\ \exp(\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{{\mathcal{Q}}})\Big)\ \in\ {\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}^{\mathbbm{C}}\ .

Hence
\Omega^{\psi}\sim_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\Omega^{\underline{\psi}}
, where

\underline{\psi}=\exp(\mathrm{i}\,\underline{\omega}_{\mathcal{Q}})

and
\varpi^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathcal{Q}}=\omega_{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Example 6.32
.

Let

\psi=\Theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{n}\ ,

for some linearly independent complex one-forms
\theta_{i}
such that
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n},\overline{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\overline{\theta}_{n}
are also linearly independent.
Suppose that there is some
X\in TM^{\mathbbm{C}}
with
X\notin T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
and some
i\in\set{1,\ldots,n}
such that
\iota_{X}\theta_{i}=0
, as well as some
a\in\mathbbm{C}
such that
X+a\,\overline{X}=:Y\in K^{\mathbbm{C}}
.
9
9

9

Locally, such a one-form
\theta_{i}
might look like
\mathrm{d}q^{i}+\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{d}s^{i}
, where
q^{i}
is a coordinate on the base
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
s^{i}
is a coordinate on the fibre
{\mathcal{F}}
. Then
X=\frac{\partial}{\partial q^{i}}+\mathrm{i}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{i}}
and
a=-1
.

It then follows that

X-Y\ \in\ \big(L\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\cap\big(\overline{L}\cap(K^{\perp})^{\mathbbm{C}}+K^{\mathbbm{C}}\big)\ .

Since
X\notin T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
it follows that
X-Y\notin T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}=K^{\mathbbm{C}}
, and so in order for such an
n
-form
\Theta
to reduce to a generalised almost complex structure on
\underline{E}
, such an
X
cannot exist.

By the arguments of Example
6.30
it follows that we can write

\Theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{k}\wedge\theta_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{n}

where
\theta_{i}\in\mathrm{Ann}(T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}})
for
i=k+1,\ldots,n
and
k=\frac{1}{2}\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{F}}
.
Suppose that
\psi
is invariant along
T{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
, and take

\mu=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{k}\ \in\ \det(T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbbm{C}})\ .

Recall that
\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}{\mathcal{Q}}=2n-2k
and consider

(6.33)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu}^{\prime}=\set{\Big(\varpi^{*}\underline{\beta}\wedge\mu\,,\,\big({\text{\footnotesize{$\int_{{\mathcal{F}}}$}}}\,\mu\wedge\overline{\mu}\big)\ \underline{\beta}\Big)\ \Big|\ \underline{\beta}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathbbm{C}}}\ .

It follows that
\Omega^{\psi}\sim_{Q({\mathcal{F}})}\Omega^{\underline{\psi}}
for

\underline{\psi}=\big({\text{\footnotesize{$\int_{\mathcal{F}}$}}}\,\mu\wedge\overline{\mu}\big)\ \underline{\Theta}\ ,

where
\varpi^{*}\underline{\Theta}=\theta_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{n}
. Thus
\psi
reduces to a generalised almost complex structure
\underline{\psi}
of type
n-k
, i.e. an ordinary almost complex structure on
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

6.3.
T-Duality and Type Change

As in Section
5.3
, let us consider again the case where
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})
is a geometric T-duality relation, hence a generalised isometry, with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
both fibred over the same manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
with bundle projections
\pi_{i}:{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{B}}
. While the following arguments can be made for an arbitrary generalised almost complex structure, for simplicity we assume integrability of the generalised almost complex structures. By a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised complex structure

{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
we mean a generalised complex structure whose
+\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
L_{1}
(and hence
-\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
\overline{L}_{1}
and associated spinor
\psi_{1}
) is
D_{1}
-invariant as in Definition
4.59
. An immediate consequence of Theorem
5.39
and Remark
6.14
is

Proposition 6.34
.

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised complex structure on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, then there is a generalised complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
such that
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1},{\mathcal{J}}_{1})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2},{\mathcal{J}}_{2})
is a generalised complex relation.

Proof.

The existence of a generalised almost complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is assured by Theorem
5.39
. From Section
5.3
, there is an
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
which is a
(\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{1}},\mathrm{d}_{\underline{H}{}_{2}})
-DGO relation. The integrability of
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
then follows by Proposition
5.38
.
∎

The type of a generalised complex structure can be determined by the pure spinor line bundle it generates. For instance, if
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is a generalised complex structure on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
of type
k
, then

(6.35)

\displaystyle\psi_{1}=\exp(B_{1}+\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{1})\wedge\Theta_{1}\ ,

where
B_{1}
and
\omega_{1}
are real two-forms, while
\Theta_{1}=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{k}
for some linearly independent complex one-forms
\theta_{i}
.

Let
\alpha=B_{1}+\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{1}
. At a point
c=(c_{1},c_{2})\in\underline{C}={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
, with
b=\pi_{1}(c_{1})=\pi_{2}(c_{2})\in{\mathcal{B}}
, there are decompositions

(6.36)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\dot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{\rm mix}\ ,

(6.37)

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}

\displaystyle=\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\mu_{1}\wedge\mu_{\rm mix}\ ,

where

(6.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}\ \in\ \text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}T_{b}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}&\quad,\quad\alpha_{1}\ \in\ \text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}T_{c_{1}}^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}^{\mathbbm{C}}\quad,\quad\alpha_{\rm mix}\ \in\ (\pi_{1}^{*}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}\wedge T^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}^{\mathbbm{C}})_{c_{1}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mu_{{\mathcal{B}}}\ \in\ \text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k_{1}}T_{b}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}&\quad,\quad\mu_{1}\ \in\ \text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k_{2}}T_{c_{1}}^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}^{\mathbbm{C}}\quad,\quad\mu_{\rm mix}\ \in\ \text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k_{3}}(\pi_{1}^{*}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}+T^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}^{\mathbbm{C}})_{c_{1}}\end{split}

are complex forms with
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
non-negative integers. As previously, an overdot denotes the pullback by
\pi_{1}^{*}
while a double overdot will denote the pullback by
\pi_{2}^{*}
. Note that

k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}

is the type of
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
.

Proposition 6.39
.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
be a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised complex structure on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
. The type of the T-dual generalised complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
is

(6.40)

\displaystyle{\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{2})={\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{1})-k_{3}+m_{3}

where
m_{3}
is the smallest positive integer such that
\alpha_{\rm mix}^{m_{3}}\neq 0
.
The pure spinor associated to
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is given by

(6.41)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\exp(B_{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{2})\wedge\Theta_{2}\ ,

where the real two-forms
B_{2}
and
\omega_{2}
and the complex form
\Theta_{2}
are given by Equation (
6.54
) below.

Proof.

We can write

(6.42)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm mix}

\displaystyle=\dot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge f_{\alpha}^{1}+\cdots+\dot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\wedge f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\ ,

(6.43)

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm mix}

\displaystyle=(\dot{{b}}_{\mu}^{1}+{f}_{\mu}^{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\dot{{b}}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}+{f}_{\mu}^{k_{3}})\ ,

for
b_{\alpha}^{i},b_{\mu}^{j}\in T_{b}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
and
f_{\alpha}^{i},f_{\mu}^{j}\in{\mathscr{G}}^{\star}_{1}{}_{c_{1}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
for
i=1,\ldots,m_{3}
and
j=1,\ldots,k_{3}
, where
{\mathscr{G}}_{1}^{\star}=\mathtt{p}_{T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}\,\mathtt{p}_{1}({\mathscr{G}})
as in the construction of the
R
-Clifford relation
{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
from Section
5.3
.

Choose
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i},\widehat{\alpha}_{1},\widehat{{f}}_{\mu}^{\,i}\in\mathtt{p}_{1}({\mathscr{G}}^{\mathbbm{C}})_{c_{1}}\cap\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{\bullet}T_{c_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}^{\mathbbm{C}}
such that

(6.44)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{1}\cdot\mu_{1}=\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}\cdot f_{\alpha}^{i}=\widehat{\alpha}_{1}\cdot\alpha_{1}=\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,i}\cdot f_{\mu}^{i}=1\ ,

while all other Clifford actions among these elements are zero, and moreover
\widehat{\mu}_{1}\cdot\overline{\mu}_{1}=0
. Let also
\widehat{\alpha}_{\rm mix}
be given by exchanging
f_{\alpha}^{i}
with
\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}
and
\dot{b}^{i}_{\alpha}
with
\ddot{b}^{i}_{\alpha}
in
\alpha_{\rm mix}
, for
i=1,\ldots,m_{3}
, and similarly for
\widehat{\mu}_{\rm mix}
.

Let
m_{2}\in\mathbbm{N}
be the smallest natural number such that
\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}\neq 0
, and set

(6.45)

\displaystyle\lambda=\frac{1}{m_{2}!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}\wedge f_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\wedge\mu_{1}\wedge\overline{\mu}_{1}\wedge f_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\ .

Since
\psi_{1}
defines a
D_{1}
-invariant generalised complex structure, it follows that
\lambda
defines a
D_{1}
-invariant fibre volume form on the tangent distribution
D_{1}=T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
.
The considerations above then enable a factorisation
\psi_{1}=\widehat{\psi}\cdot\lambda
thanks to

Lemma 6.46
.

The forms defined above satisfy

(1)

\displaystyle\exp(\widehat{\alpha}_{1})\cdot\frac{1}{m_{2}!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}=\exp(\alpha_{1})\ ,

(2)

\exp(\widehat{\alpha}_{\rm mix})\cdot(f_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}})=(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}+f_{\alpha}^{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}+f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}})\ ,

(3)

\widehat{\mu}_{1}\cdot(\mu_{1}\wedge\overline{\mu}_{1})=\overline{\mu}_{1}\ ,
and

(4)

\widehat{\mu}_{\rm mix}\cdot(f_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})=\exp(\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{1}\wedge f_{\mu}^{1}+\cdots+\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})\ .

Proof.

(1) Firstly, for each natural number
j\leqslant m_{2}

(6.47)

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{1}^{j}\cdot\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}=m_{2}\,j\ \widehat{\alpha}_{1}^{j-1}\cdot\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}-1}=\cdots=\frac{m_{2}!\,j!}{(m_{2}-j)!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}-j}\ .

Thus

(6.48)

\displaystyle\exp(\widehat{\alpha}_{1})\cdot\frac{1}{m_{2}!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}=\sum_{j=0}^{m_{2}}\,\frac{1}{j!}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{1}^{j}\cdot\frac{1}{m_{2}!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}}=\sum_{j=0}^{m_{2}}\,\frac{1}{m_{2}!\,j!}\,\frac{m_{2}!\,j!}{(m_{2}-j)!}\,\alpha_{1}^{m_{2}-j}=\exp(\alpha_{1})\ .

(2) We compute

(6.49)

\displaystyle\exp(\widehat{\alpha}_{\rm mix})\cdot(f_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}})

\displaystyle=\exp(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,1})\wedge\cdots\wedge\exp(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,m_{3}})\cdot(f_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}})

\displaystyle=\big(\exp(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,1})\cdot f_{\alpha}^{1}\,\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\exp(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,m_{3}})\cdot f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\,\big)

\displaystyle=\big((1+\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,1})\cdot f_{\alpha}^{1}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big((1+\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\wedge\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,m_{3}})\cdot f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}\big)

\displaystyle=(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}+f_{\alpha}^{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}+f_{\alpha}^{m_{3}})\ ,

where we use the fact that
\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}\cdot\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{j}=0
for all
i,j
and
\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}\cdot f_{\alpha}^{j}=0
when
i\neq j
to commute the exponentials.

(3) Follows immediately.

(4) Similarly to (2) we compute

(6.50)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{\rm mix}\cdot(f_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})

\displaystyle=(\ddot{b}_{\mu}+\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}+\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,k_{3}})\cdot(f_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})

\displaystyle=\big((\ddot{b}_{\mu}+\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,1})\cdot f_{\mu}^{1}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big((\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}+\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,k_{3}})\cdot f_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\big)

\displaystyle=(1+\ddot{b}_{\mu}\wedge f_{\mu}^{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(1+\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})

\displaystyle=\exp(\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{1}\wedge f_{\mu}^{1}+\cdots+\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\wedge f_{\mu}^{k_{3}})\ ,

as required.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
6.39
.
By Proposition
5.3
, there is a pure spinor
\psi_{2}
such that
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\in{\mathsf{S}}^{\mathbbm{C}}_{R\,c}
.
From Lemma
6.46
we obtain

(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})=(\widehat{\psi},\psi_{2})\cdot(\lambda,1)\ ,

where

(6.51)

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}=\exp(\dot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\widehat{\alpha}_{1}+\widehat{\alpha}_{\rm mix})\wedge\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\widehat{\mu}_{1}\wedge\widehat{\mu}_{\rm mix}\ .

Note that
(\dot{\beta},\ddot{\beta})\in\mathsf{Cl}(R)_{c}
for
\beta\in T_{b}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}
, by the splitting (
5.49
).
It follows that

(6.52)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}

\displaystyle=\exp(\ddot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}})\wedge\exp(\alpha_{2})\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}+\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}+\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,m_{3}})

\displaystyle\wedge\ddot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\overline{\mu}_{2}\wedge\exp(\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,1}+\cdots+\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\wedge\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,k_{3}})

for some
\alpha_{2}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}T_{c_{2}}^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}^{\mathbbm{C}}
and
\mu_{2}\in\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{k_{2}}T^{*}_{c_{2}}\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}^{\mathbbm{C}}
such that
\widehat{\alpha}_{1}\sim_{R}\alpha_{2}
and
\widehat{\mu}_{1}\sim_{R}\mu_{2}
respectively, with
\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}
(resp.
\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,j}
) such that
\widehat{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}\sim_{R}\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,i}
(resp.
\widehat{f}_{\mu}^{\,j}\sim_{R}\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,j}
) in
\mathsf{Cl}(R)^{\mathbbm{C}}_{c}
, for
i=1,\ldots,m_{3}
and
j=1,\ldots,k_{3}
.

Finally, we can write (
6.52
) in the form

(6.53)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\exp(B_{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{2})\wedge\Theta_{2}\ ,

where we have made the definitions

(6.54)

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\rm Re}(\ddot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}})\ ,

\displaystyle\omega_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\rm Im}(\ddot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}})\ ,

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mu_{\alpha_{\rm mix}}\wedge\ddot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\overline{\mu}_{2}\ ,

with

(6.55)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}}

\displaystyle=\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{1}\wedge\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,1}+\cdots+\ddot{b}_{\mu}^{k_{3}}\wedge\widetilde{f}_{\mu}^{\,k_{3}}\ ,

(6.56)

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha_{\rm mix}}

\displaystyle=(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{1}+\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\ddot{b}_{\alpha}^{m_{3}}+\widetilde{f}_{\alpha}^{\,m_{3}})\ .

Hence the type of
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is
k_{1}+k_{2}+m_{3}
, and the result follows.
∎

Remark 6.57
.

Since
\lambda
defines a fibre volume form on the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
, it follows that

d:=\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(D_{1})=2k_{2}+k_{3}+2m_{2}+m_{3}\ .

As
{\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{2})={\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{1})-k_{3}+m_{3}
, we then find

(6.58)

\displaystyle{\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{2})={\rm type}({\mathcal{J}}_{1})+2(k_{2}+m_{2}+m_{3})-d\ .

This is in agreement with the discussion of change of type of generalised complex structures under T-duality for principle torus bundles in
[
14
, Example 4.3]
.

Example 6.59
.

Continuing in analogy with
[
14
]
, the expression (
6.52
) allows us to explicitly determine what becomes of the type in specific instances of generalised complex structures on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
. Suppose that
\dim_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})=2n
and that the foliation
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
has real codimension
n
, i.e.
\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbbm{R}}(D_{1})=n
.

\Diamond

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is purely complex and the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
are also complex, then the associated pure spinor has the form

(6.60)

\displaystyle\psi_{1}=\dot{\mu}_{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\mu_{1}\ ,

that is, there is no
\mu_{\rm mix}
component. Thus the T-dual spinor corresponding to the complex structure
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is given by

(6.61)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\ddot{\mu}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\wedge\overline{\mu}_{2}\ .

Hence
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is also a purely complex structure.

\Diamond

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is a complex structure given by
J_{1}
and the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
are real (i.e.
J_{1}(T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1})\cap T\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}=\set{0}
), then

(6.62)

\displaystyle\psi_{1}=\mu_{\rm mix}

and hence

(6.63)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\exp\big({\rm Re}(\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}})+\mathrm{i}\,{\rm Im}(\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}})\big)\ .

Thus
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is symplectic with a
B
-twist. Since
\alpha_{\mu_{\rm mix}}
has always a component in
\pi_{2}^{*}T^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathbbm{C}}
, the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{2}
define Lagrangian submanifolds with respect to this symplectic structure.

\Diamond

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is purely symplectic (with a
B
-twist) and the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
are symplectic, then

\psi_{1}=\exp(\dot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{1})\ ,

and hence

\psi_{2}=\exp(\ddot{\alpha}_{\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{2})

is also symplectic.

\Diamond

If
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
is symplectic and the leaves of
\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
are Lagrangian, then

\psi_{1}=\exp(\alpha_{\rm mix})\ ,

and hence

\psi_{2}=\mu_{\alpha_{\rm mix}}\ ,

which therefore has complex type.

We thus recover and generalise the results of
[
14
, Example 4.3, Table 1]
in the case of torus bundles, which we summarize here in the table

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}

\underline{{\mathcal{F}}}{}_{1}
